# General backlog — ranked, with triggers

Everything outstanding across the plan, the harness, and follow-ups discovered during execution — ranked by take-order (value weighted by effort), each with the **trigger** that makes it actionable. Component candidates live in [`component-backlog.md`](./component-backlog.md) (own queue, own triggers); this list covers everything else. Status ground truth: plan Phases 0–3 are merged (PR #8), and Phase 4's architecture, migrations, and the 2026-09-01/02 incident-response rounds (PRs #10–#35) have since landed on `main` — see [`plan.md`](./plan.md) for the current phase-by-phase status.

## Fresh-session order (2026-09-04)

The table below is ranked by take-order already; this block is a **recommendation for what a fresh session should pick up first**, layered on top of it — easy to reverse, not a renumbering of the rows. Rationale: fix what's broken before building more, cheap infrastructure before expensive components, and keep CI-gated items visibly separate from everything else since they aren't sequenced at all (they wait on the user lifting the CI freeze).

**Environment note (2026-09-04):** this sandbox got a working Playwright container for the first time this session — `dx` 0.7.9 and the wasm32 target installed, chromium/firefox/webkit browsers all installed, `dx build --web --release` succeeds. Three `dx`-managed tool downloads had to be placed by hand because `dx`'s HTTP client does not trust the agent proxy's CA (`invalid peer certificate: UnknownIssuer`): esbuild 0.27.3 and binaryen/wasm-opt into `~/.local/share/.dx/tools/`, and wasm-bindgen compiled from source. A fresh session hitting the same proxy should expect the identical failure and know the workaround rather than rediscovering it. A firefox+webkit calibration run (row 4) was started in this same sandbox and was **deliberately stopped partway** (178/1094, 14 failures recorded in firefox) as a scope decision — it is user-gated group-6 work (item 5 below) and was blocking the queue. Its outcome is genuinely incomplete, not merely unreported: a fresh session resuming calibration should rerun it cleanly rather than try to pick up where it left off.

**Environment note (2026-09-18):** a fresh container again had no `dx`. GitHub over HTTPS is blocked by this environment's egress policy (403 on `api.github.com` and on release downloads) but `git fetch` from github.com works — forks and upstream `refs/pull/N/head` included — and crates.io works, so `cargo install dioxus-cli --version 0.7.9 --locked` (~20 min on 4 cores) is the route. `dx` then self-compiles wasm-bindgen from source when its GitHub download fails (144 s, into `~/.local/share/.dx/tools/wasm-bindgen-0.2.121/`), but its own reqwest client (webpki roots) rejects the proxy CA even for `NO_PROXY` hosts, so the other two tools were placed by hand at the paths `dx` checks first: esbuild 0.27.3 from the npm tgz (curl trusts the CA) at `~/.local/share/.dx/tools/esbuild-0.27.3/esbuild`, and `wasm-opt` at `~/.local/share/.dx/tools/binaryen-129/bin/wasm-opt` as a wrapper over the npm `binaryen@129.0.0` JS/wasm build (Ubuntu's apt `binaryen` is version 108 — it accepts every flag `dx` passes but predates the sign-ext/multivalue defaults rustc 1.94 emits, so it was not used). Parallel lanes ran in git worktrees sharing one cargo build directory (`~/.cargo/config.toml` `build.target-dir`), which reuses compiled dependencies across worktrees; cargo serialises on its lock, so "Blocking waiting for file lock" is expected, not a hang.

**New lints and tools landed this session, all wired to rows 31b/32:** two mechanical-check scripts joined `scripts/check-preview-composition.sh` and `scripts/check-cfg-axis.sh` — `scripts/check-dx-class-prefix.sh` (every themed component's shipped classes must be namespaced `dx-<component>[-...]`, the collision-safety property `#[css_module]` hashing used to provide, now that row 32 has dropped it) and `scripts/check-css-literals.sh` (a themed stylesheet may not hard-code a value that exactly matches a row-31a design token, enforcing row 31b's migration against drift). Both should run alongside the existing gates before any CSS-touching change lands. Two Playwright files are TOOLS rather than oracles, not run as part of the normal suite: `playwright/dx-class-migration.spec.ts` (generic per-component delivery + unhashed-class assertions — this is the one that caught `sidebar`'s stylesheet not reaching its iframe-embedded demo, and now also the calendar/date_picker one row 51 fixed) and `playwright/computed-style-snapshot.spec.ts` (skipped unless `DX_SNAPSHOT` is set; captures computed styles across every themed page so a literal→token migration can be proven value-preserving by diff rather than by eye).

**Update, later the same date:** priorities 1 and 2 below have now both landed — see rows 10, 26, 27, 50, 51, 52, 53, and 54 for the individual accounts. This block's numbering stays as a record of the order actually taken, rather than being renumbered out from under the rows it points at; the paragraph after the table says what a fresh session should pick up now that both are clear. **Verified by a full Chromium run: 162 passed / 3 failed.** Of the 3: two are `top-layer.spec.ts` Rule 8 cases, pre-existing and failing 100% in this sandbox — row 21's own documented anchor-freeze, not a regression from anything landed this round; the third was a genuine `button_group` axe `button-name` violation (the two icon-only buttons — see row 27), fixed in the same round, so the run's own 3-failed count already reflects a defect this pass caught and closed, not one still outstanding.

1. **Known bugs — empty of blockers, all the way through.** Row 26 (route DOM leak) closed as not-reproduced across chromium/firefox/webkit, and its own `dropdown-menu.spec.ts` workaround is now removed, since the workaround's stated mechanism was disproved, not merely unconfirmed. Alongside this, a **new, separate item landed**: the menu-family role consolidation's stage 1 (row 52, Navbar now reads its menu-pattern roles from `menu_semantics` instead of hand-writing a fourth set), with a stage-2 assessment recorded but explicitly **not built** (row 53 — a scoped three-band extraction, not a single shared submenu component).
3. **Row 27 — landed.** All nine styled-only components (Alert, Breadcrumb, Button Group, Empty, Field, Input Group, Kbd, Spinner, Native Select), authored against the row-31a tokens in the row-32 styling system, checked against both new lints above, each with its own smoke + axe spec. One axe finding along the way, fixed the same round: see row 27 for the two icon-only `button_group` buttons axe's `button-name` rule caught.

**What a fresh session should pick up next, now that 1 and 2 above are clear:**

## 2026-09-18 — queue evaluation and what was taken

A fresh session was asked to evaluate this queue and, if it concurred, continue. **Verdict: concur, with three corrections**, all applied the same day (evidence on the rows they point at):

1. **The ordering rule stands and the queue state was accurate.** Rows 55–65 (PRs #51–57) were user-reported docs-site and Input OTP fixes; the known-bugs step is empty again; Phase 5 is complete (`plan.md`'s header still said "partially done" — refreshed).
2. **The cheap-primitives wave (item 6 above) omitted its two cheapest members.** `component-backlog.md` rates Table and Data Table at S each, below the M-rated Drawer/Navigation Menu/Resizable the wave names; by this queue's own "cheap before expensive" rule they go first, and needing no primitive they form a disjoint lane. Taken.
3. **Six "Ready", S-rated rows idled behind the wave with no stated reason** — rows 7, 8, 9, 11, 12, 19 — whose file sets are disjoint from the wave's, so under `CLAUDE.md`'s lane rule they run *alongside* it, not after it. Taken as parallel lanes.
4. **An unstated dependency: row 7 depends on row 19.** Row 7 was marked "Unblocked", but row 19 records that the web arm's `hidePopover()` fires before any close animation can play, so the fade-out CSS alone is invisible there. Sequenced 19 → 7 in one lane.
5. **Upstream moved** (row 66) — the first time since the research baseline; one cosmetic fix adopted, two `use_animated_open` fixes already subsumed here.
6. **Unchanged:** the user-gated rows (4, 6, 14, 22, 33; 15, 23; 28, 29, 30, 40, 41b) and the deferred Carousel/Chart. `plan.md` 5.3 is re-derive-or-drop, not pending: its source branch exists neither in this clone nor on the fork remote.
7. **Gate state on unmodified `8cd8dbf`, captured before any lane started:** `cargo test --workspace` 120 passed, `cargo fmt --check` clean, the four `scripts/check-*.sh` guards clean; **clippy red** (five `doc_lazy_continuation` errors in `menu_sub.rs`, known from row 53's account); **stylelint red** (nine errors across the `input_otp`, `select` and `sidebar` stylesheets, introduced by PRs #51–57 — row 67). Same class as row 48: with every workflow `workflow_dispatch`-only, nothing catches a red gate. Row 33 (CI reactivation) is the construction that closes the class and stays user-gated; the one available now is an explicit pre-commit gate list in `CLAUDE.md`, added this session.

**Outcome.** What landed: rows 7, 8, 9, 11, 12, 19 (Tooltip/HoverCard/Popover close-fade, Select `role=combobox` trigger + listbox scroll lock, menu-family typeahead, the portal/Toggle-class/`CalendarDayState` public-API trio) plus the five-lane cheap-primitives wave — Table, Data Table, Drawer, Navigation Menu, Resizable (own rows in `component-backlog.md`) — 91 files, +9930/−134 against the session's starting point `8cd8dbf`. Gate state after the session: **all green** in an isolated build (`cargo test --workspace`, `fmt --check`, the four `check-*.sh` guards, `check --workspace --all-targets`, `check --features web`, `clippy --workspace --tests --examples -- -D warnings`, `stylelint`, and `cargo doc -p dioxus-primitives`/`-p preview --no-deps --document-private-items`, both plain and `--features web`). Before the session, on unmodified `8cd8dbf`: clippy was red (5 pre-existing `doc_lazy_continuation` errors in `menu_sub.rs`), rustdoc was red (12 broken/private intra-doc-link errors), and stylelint was red (9 errors from PRs #51-57) — all three closed this session (row 67); two more `preview` warnings and 10 more intra-doc-link errors this session's own new lanes introduced, plus 4 more only visible once the `--features web` doc build was run for the first time, were closed the same way (row 67 has the full accounting). Browser verification state: targeted Playwright runs are green for all new work, including the round-3 fixes for the Drawer drag hang (row 73), the Navigation Menu first-render panic and name collisions (row 74), and the duplicate-id/caption-locator strict-mode violations (row 75); the pre-existing 2026-09-18 baseline's groups B (axe readiness race), C (sidebar-interception timeouts) and D (Rule 11 self-overlap) are all fixed and confirmed green (rows 69, 70, 71). Groups A1 (row 21's date-picker/ColorPicker Chromium anchor-position engine freeze) and A2 (row 22's SSG lane not running — connection-refused, "not run" rather than "failed") remain red, but **environment-only** — row 21's own conclusion is a browser-engine bug, not this repo's code, and A2 needs an SSG server this lane never started; neither is a regression from anything landed this session. **Full-suite totals (round 3, one invocation, the first to finish completely — round 2's two attempts both degraded from Chromium/CDP contention before finishing):** 747 passed / 10 failed / 11 skipped of 768 (up from the baseline's 694 entries — +74 from this session's new specs), ~15.6 minutes. Of the 10: **2 were a genuine new defect** — `top-layer-ink.spec.ts`'s "select" surface (light + dark color scheme) hung the 5-minute ceiling on a stale `page.getByRole("button")` locator that row 8's `role="combobox"` change (landed before this round) had already made permanently non-matching; this was the first full run since row 8 landed to ever exercise it. A third miss of the same class row 8's own account already names two instances of. Fixed same day, commit `6c3380e` (one-line locator swap); both variants verified passing in 1.4s each, was 5.0m. **The other 8 are environment-only, not a regression:** 5 match baseline group A2 exactly (`hydration-parity.spec.ts`, SSG lane never started in this dev-server-only run); 2 match baseline group A1 (date-picker's "anchors the popup..." and the ColorPicker Rule 8 scroll case) — its third A1 member, date-picker's scroll-offset case, passed this run, consistent with row 21's own account of a load-timing-correlated browser bug rather than a deterministic one; 1 is `drag_and_drop_list.spec.ts`'s "cancelled mouse drag" case, confirmed flaky by reproduction (5/5 green in isolation) — one task's assignee-initials `AvatarFallback` text appearing mid-test once its external, GitHub-hosted avatar image finishes failing to load under this sandbox's proxied network, racing the two `itemText()` polls the test takes before and after the (correctly no-op) cancelled drag.

| 10 | **Phase 5** — collision detection | **5.1 REFRAMED by the 2026-09-01 bits-ui/Radix source comparison, FLIP sub-problem landed same day, PR #17**: both delegate to Floating UI (identical flip/shift/size/arrow/hide pipelines; bits-ui is a line-for-line Svelte port of Radix's Popper). Since CSS Anchor Positioning is now in our stack, `position-try-fallbacks: flip-block, flip-inline` covers the FLIP sub-problem natively (Chrome/Edge 125+, FF 147+, Safari 26+), zero JS, additive to the existing @supports blocks — landed, and `use_anchor_position_fallback` makes the same flip decision from viewport math on engines without native support. **2026-09-03: the JS-fallback path's first piece of shift also landed** (user device report — the home page widget-masonry `ColorPicker` clipping against a small screen's edge; `top-layer.spec.ts` Rule 12): `use_anchor_position_fallback` now clamps `target.left` into the viewport with a small edge margin, on top of the existing flip decision, closing the gap for `side="top"`/`"bottom"` placements (whose horizontal position comes from `align` alone and was never checked against the viewport on *either* engine before this). **2026-09-04: the vertical shift and size clamp landed too**, in `use_anchor_position_fallback` — new **Rule 15** in `top-layer.spec.ts`, passing. Two pieces, the exact mirror of the horizontal fix: (1) a `side="left"`/`"right"` placement's *vertical* position — like a `side="top"`/`"bottom"` placement's horizontal one — came only from `align` and was never checked against the viewport, clamped into `[EDGE_MARGIN, vh - EDGE_MARGIN - ch]` the same way; (2) when the content's natural height exceeds `vh - 2*EDGE_MARGIN`, an inline `max-height`/`overflow-y: auto` is set (and `box-sizing: border-box` forced alongside it, since `offsetHeight` is always border-box but `max-height` is defined relative to whatever `box-sizing` the element carries — under `content-box`, padding/border would silently under-clamp), cleared again once no longer needed. Deliberately **not** mirrored into the CSS `@supports` contract, and the reasoning is worth keeping on record: `position-try-fallbacks` has no shift primitive and no shipped primitive shrinks an anchor box to fit, so a conforming engine has the identical latent gap — this is not Firefox/WebKit-specific, it is a real gap in the standard as shipped today. The size clamp uses an inline style rather than the width contract's `--dx-anchor-width` custom-property approach (row 47) specifically because a zero-specificity `:where()` rule can never beat a component's own `overflow: visible` (`select/style.css`'s own comment: "never scrolls ... collision-aware ... positioning belongs upstream" — this clamp is that upstream). **2026-09-14: 5.2, `ContextMenu`'s point-anchor clamp, has now landed too — the one piece of this row that was left deliberately open.** `ContextMenu` opens at a raw click/long-press point (`ContextMenuCtx::position`, set by `ContextMenuTrigger`'s mouse `contextmenu` handler and its manual touch/pen long-press timer) with no anchor element at all — `use_anchor_position_fallback` above cannot cover it regardless of engine, not because of a Firefox/WebKit gap but because that function is keyed on resolving an anchor element via `document.querySelector('[style*="anchor-name: ..."]')`, and `ContextMenuContentRendered` never wires up an `anchor-name` for this content in the first place (see that component's own doc, the `manual`-vs-`auto` section, for why: there is no persistent trigger to key positioning off of). Closed by a **new, parallel construction** in `top_layer.rs`, `use_point_anchor_clamp` — not a branch threaded through `use_anchor_position_fallback` itself: that function's script is already long, heavily cross-referenced, and execution-verified around anchor-relative `place()`/`matches()`/flip machinery this case has no use for, so adding a no-anchor special case there was judged a real risk to its own passing oracle coverage (rules 5-7, 9, 11-15) for a case that shares only the final clamp *arithmetic*. The new hook reuses exactly that arithmetic — the same `EDGE_MARGIN` (4px) convention, the same `Math.max` viewport-too-small-to-fit-at-all fallback, the same `border-box`-forced max-height/`overflow-y: auto` clamp `use_anchor_position_fallback`'s own 2026-09-04 vertical-shift-and-size-clamp addendum above describes — against the raw `(x, y)` point directly instead of an anchor rect, measuring `ContextMenuContentRendered`'s own rendered size once it's promoted to the top layer (called right after `use_popover_sync` in that component, the same ordering requirement `use_anchor_position_fallback` documents for its own call sites). No scroll/resize tracking and no multi-frame settle loop, unlike `use_anchor_position_fallback`: `ContextMenuContent`'s items are ordinary synchronously-rendered children, not `DatePickerPopover`'s asynchronously-growing calendar grid, and the menu already suppresses page scroll while open (its own pre-existing wheel/touchmove effect) rather than following it. `menu_sub.rs`'s `ContextMenuSub`/`ContextMenuSubTrigger`/`ContextMenuSubContent` were checked and confirmed **not** to share this bug: a submenu anchors to its own `ContextMenuSubTrigger` (an ordinary menu item sitting inside the already-positioned root content), the same anchor-relative shape `DropdownMenuSub` uses, so it already goes through `use_anchor_position_fallback` like every other anchored overlay — untouched by this fix. Regression oracle: two new `playwright/context-menu.spec.ts` cases ("clamps the menu into the viewport for a mouse right-click near a corner" / "... for a touch long-press near a corner"), covering both of `ContextMenuTrigger`'s `position.set(...)` call sites — not `top-layer.spec.ts`, whose own header doc explicitly disclaims this exact case (Migration A slice 2/3's note: "Phase 5.2's point-anchor case is explicitly out of scope for this file"). **Verified by actual execution (Chromium, this sandbox), 2026-09-14:** `cargo check --workspace` and `cargo fmt --check` clean (no new warnings beyond the two pre-existing, unrelated ones already on `main`); `cargo clippy` raises no new warnings anywhere outside the confirmed pre-existing, unrelated `menu_sub.rs` doc-lint failures (present unchanged on `main` at `124c4d5`). The two new cases pass. **A third case regressed and was root-caused, not just re-run until green:** the pre-existing `menu lands at the tap coordinates on touch long-press` case started failing after this fix landed (menu Y off by ~187px) — verified by a direct baseline comparison (same test, same browser, run against `124c4d5` before this fix: passes) that this was a real, reproducible difference correlated with the fix, not a flake. Root cause, confirmed by instrumented execution: **not a bug in `use_point_anchor_clamp`.** The test's own premise (push the trigger down with `padding-top: 300px` on `<main>` so the tap point sits "away from any edge") had gone stale — on this component's current preview-page chrome, that padding places the trigger's center at `y ≈ 735` against a 720px-tall default viewport, 15px *past* the bottom edge, not away from it. That was already true on `124c4d5`, before `use_point_anchor_clamp` existed — nothing corrected it yet, so the unclamped menu simply rendered past the fold and the test's tap-coordinate assertion passed vacuously (it never checked on-screen visibility, only that the menu's top-left matched the tap point). `use_point_anchor_clamp` landing gave the clamp something to correct for the first time, and it correctly pulls the menu back on-screen (measured: `vh=720`, `tapY≈735`, content height `168`, for which `top + ch > vh - EDGE_MARGIN` is genuinely true) — a real, intended behavior change exposing a latent bad premise in the test, not a regression in the fix. Fixed by pinning the trigger to an explicit `position: fixed; top: 120px; left: 160px` instead of relying on ambient padding, making "nowhere near an edge" true by construction rather than by an increasingly-stale coincidence. **All 13 cases in `context-menu.spec.ts` pass together** after this correction. A firefox/webkit run has not been attempted. Still open in this row: only the acknowledged conforming-engine CSS gap described above (no CSS primitive exists for the point-anchor case either — a raw click point was never something `anchor()` could place, so there is nothing for a future `@supports` rule to cover here even in principle) | S (flip, done) + S (JS-fallback shift, done) + S (vertical/size clamp, done) + S (5.2, ContextMenu point-anchor, done 2026-09-14) | Keep the CSS clamp (5.3) regardless — still pending, see `recommended-implementations.md` §6. Two adoptable ideas from the comparison, not yet taken: bits-ui's debounced nested-lock cleanup (~24ms, survives same-tick unmount/remount races), and Radix's right-click carve-out for backdrop dismiss |

A submenu-content-has-no-portal fact (`DropdownMenuSubContent`/`ContextMenuSubContent` render nested inside their root menu's own content, confirmed via `scripts/inspect.mjs`) is a testing-scope note for future submenu tests, not a defect. **Comparative finding:** shadcn's own `new-york-v4` stylesheets carry essentially no RTL support of their own (zero logical CSS utilities, `rtl:` in only 2 of 62 files) — the real RTL behaviour this row ports lives in Radix's JS, not shadcn's CSS, confirming this row's direction-aware-Rust-plus-logical-CSS split as the right shape. **Correction to a prior cross-reference:** `plan.md`'s Phase 6 status line and `rtl.spec.ts`'s own header both cite "`conformance-harness.md`'s queue item 8" for the fixture-decision gap this row closes — verified against the current file and found inaccurate; see `plan.md`'s own 2026-09-19 status paragraph for the correction. The fixture decision above is real and landed regardless of that mis-citation. **Correction to record, 2026-09-25 (round 8):** this row's own "Follow-ups, not built this round" sentence above — "`navigation_menu`/`drawer`/`command`/`date_picker` have no RTL support yet" — is not a shadcn/ui parity gap; the user asked directly whether it was one, and it is not. This row's own "Comparative finding" already established that shadcn's own `new-york-v4` stylesheets carry essentially no RTL support of their own (zero logical CSS utilities, `rtl:` in only 2 of 62 files) — parity with shadcn does not require RTL logic for any component. All four already have logical-property CSS from this row's own `3ce62f5` conversion pass; what they lack is direction-aware keyboard logic plus an `rtl` fixture page, the same shape this row built for the other 13. For two of the four there may be nothing meaningful to flip at all: `Command`'s list is a vertical stack (no left/right concept), and `Drawer`'s `side` prop is a physical-geometry fact, not a reading-direction one (the same reasoning this row's own CSS pass already applied to keep `data-side` physical rather than logical). Kept as an optional beyond-parity item, not scheduled |

Regression test: a new shared sampler `playwright/assert-fade-out.ts` (modeled on `assert-close-animation.ts` + `accordion-animation.spec.ts`'s rAF sampling loop) plus one test per component (`tooltip.spec.ts`/`hover-card.spec.ts`/`popover.spec.ts`) samples the closing element's `opacity`/`display` every frame and asserts it stays displayed with decreasing opacity for a few frames, reaches ~0 before unmount, and unmounts within ~1.5s — demonstrated RED on pre-fix `tooltip.rs`, GREEN restored, during integration round 1. **Surprise found integrating:** `popover.spec.ts`'s non-modal case drives the `top_layer` oracle fixture's raw `#stack-popover-*` instance, which deliberately carries no themed CSS at all — there is no close animation to observe there regardless of this fix (`use_animated_open` correctly unmounts immediately with nothing to hold), so that one sub-case cannot pass against its current test subject. | 31 | **Design tokens (option A) — split in two (user decision 2026-09-03).** **31a — LANDED 2026-09-04.** Token set added to `preview/assets/dx-components-theme.css` (today colour + dark-mode only): type scale (9 steps), spacing scale (16 steps on the 4px grid, matching Tailwind v4's default), radius scale (8), elevation/shadow (5, dark-mode-off per existing convention), motion durations/easings, breakpoints (reference-only — `var()` does not resolve inside `@media`, stated in-file), z-index scale (9 steps, all 25 existing literals map 1:1) — as CSS custom properties, which survive any outcome of row 32 (Tailwind v4's `@theme` block *is* CSS variables). Every scale was derived from a literal survey of the 46 component stylesheets, not invented; `line-height`/`font-weight` were deliberately NOT tokenised (no clean clusters emerged). The touch floor (row 30) is folded into `--dx-text-base`; row 39's ≥4.5:1 contrast requirement is stated in-file as a rule for future token pairs. Also landed: a small **generated utility sheet** — new `scripts/generate-dx-utilities.js` emits 336 atomic `dx-`-prefixed classes (spacing, gap, flex/grid basics, text sizes, radius, shadow) to `preview/assets/dx-utilities.css` from the token file, with a `--check` staleness mode, so it cannot drift; variants (`hover:`, `md:`, `data-[state]:`) stay in component rules, not in the generator. A shared `:where([data-dx-interactive])` interaction-rules layer (hover opacity / focus-visible ring / disabled opacity / reduced-motion, driven by tokens) was also added but is **scaffolding only**: nothing renders the `data-dx-interactive` attribute yet (wiring it onto components is `primitives/src` work), so the hover-opacity and disabled-opacity rules are inert by design; reduced-motion IS live today, via the same tag/role selector list `main.css` already uses for `touch-action`. **31b — LANDED 2026-09-04.** 683 substitutions across 44 component stylesheets, EXACT-value matches only: a literal is replaced solely when it equals a token's value in the token's own unit (`8px` where a token says `0.5rem` is left alone and logged as an informational note, never auto-swapped — that would be a real behaviour change under a consumer overriding the root font-size, a design decision rather than a mechanical one). Verified value-preserving by measurement, not assertion: `playwright/computed-style-snapshot.spec.ts` (a TOOL, skipped unless `DX_SNAPSHOT` is set) captured 24 computed properties across all 7,774 `dx-` elements on the 46 component pages before and after the migration — the diff is exactly one entry, `dx-progress-indicator`'s width, which re-samples to 34/54/32/54px on a single *unchanged* build because that demo animates, so zero real differences. `scripts/check-css-literals.sh` (new lint) enforces this going forward: exact-unit matches only, property-family-aware token suggestions (so `gap: 1rem` is never suggested as `var(--dx-text-base)` just because `1rem` also happens to be a type-scale step), scoped to the root `component.json`'s `members` (excludes `top_layer`, an oracle fixture whose hand-tuned geometry is deliberately literal). Several traps were correctly declined rather than tokenised: `tabs`' dark-branch inset ring, `dialog`/`sheet`/`toast`'s shadows with no dark/light wrapper (tokenising would have silently dropped the shadow in dark mode), `999px` (not `9999px`), and non-`--dx-ring` focus rings (3px, `color-mix()`, or component-local). **Important correction, discovered mid-round, worth recording as a third instance of a known bug class:** a first attempt consolidated 11 components' `:focus-visible` rings into the shared interaction layer by naming their classes directly in the global theme sheet (`.dx-checkbox:focus-visible`, …). That cannot work: `#[css_module]` hashes each class in **both** the DOM and the component's own stylesheet, so an unhashed selector written in a *different*, global sheet never matches — confirmed against the built artifact (the wasm carries `dx-alert-dialog-cancel-e59ac016` while the theme sheet said `.dx-alert-dialog-cancel`). Shipping it would have silently removed the focus ring from 11 controls (WCAG 2.4.7), so it did not ship: each component instead keeps its own `:focus-visible` rule, rewritten against the new `--dx-ring` token. Consolidation becomes possible once row 32 drops `css_module` hashing, or once `data-dx-interactive` is wired onto components. This is the **third** instance of the `css_module` hashing/scoping class — after row 32's own originating instance (the shared anchor-positioning `@supports` workaround) and row 47's (`select/style.css`'s dead `@supports` block) — cross-reference all three | **31a: landed 2026-09-04. 31b: landed 2026-09-04** | 31a S–M (done); 31b M (done) | Evidence and per-axis counts: the 2026-09-02 design-system research (`recommended-implementations.md`, design-system note); non-breaking for consumers either way (`dx components add` copies the same theme file, richer) |
| 32 | **Styling engine — DECIDED 2026-09-03, LANDED 2026-09-04: (b) plain `dx-`-prefixed CSS files without `css_module` hashing, on the row-31a tokens** (user agreed with the recommendation; the dignifiedquire comparison the same day reinforced it: their Tailwind setup ended up as a crate dependency with a hand-maintained safelist and stale copy-in manifests — the exact consumer trap this avoids). **Execution, in two commits:** a proof-of-concept on `checkbox`/`select` first, then the mechanical pass across the remaining 42 (43 total — the macro gone, `Styles::dx_x` → the literal `"dx-x"`, each component's own stylesheet delivered via `document::Link`/`asset!`, which dedupes on `(href, rel)` so mounting many instances still emits one `<link>`). `top_layer` is deliberately the one component left on `#[css_module]`: it is an oracle fixture rather than an installable component (outside the root `component.json` `members` list), and its `dx-anchor-*` markers are intentionally global coordination points with the stylesheet `primitives/src/top_layer.rs` injects, so unhashing it needs a deliberate decision, not a mechanical pass. New `scripts/check-dx-class-prefix.sh` enforces the collision-safety property hashing used to give — every class a themed component's own `style.css` defines must be `dx-<component>` or `dx-<component>-…`; a component still on `#[css_module]` is a warning, not a failure, so the check cannot itself block the (already-complete) migration, only guard against future drift — and it caught two real pre-existing collisions that hashing alone had been masking: `.dx-remove-button` meant two different things in `drag_and_drop_list` (26×26px) and `tag_group` (unsized), so both were renamed (`dx-drag-and-drop-list-remove-button`/`dx-tag-group-remove-button`) rather than merged; `.dx-sr-only` was byte-identical between `pagination` and `sidebar`, so it was left namespaced in both and flagged as a row-31a utility-sheet candidate. New `playwright/dx-class-migration.spec.ts` (two generic assertions run over all 43 components: some stylesheet reachable from the page defines `.dx-<component>`, and no element anywhere carries the `dx-…-<8 hex>` hashed shape) caught a real delivery bug on first run — `sidebar`'s stylesheet wasn't reaching its iframe-embedded demo — the exact silent-failure shape no role/name-selecting spec in the suite could otherwise see. The `dx-anchor-*` unhashed-marker workaround was deleted along with every migrated component's `@supports` scoping bug (`dev-docs/issues/css-module-supports-scoping.md` kept as the upstream report, since the bug still applies to any consumer still on an older `css_module`-hashing release). Rejected alternatives on record: Tailwind v4 (consumers must run Tailwind; copied components stop being editable; `tailwind_fuse`'s `TwClass`/`TwVariant` derives noted as good prior art if ever revisited), precompiled/prefixed Tailwind (consumers still can't regenerate CSS from the copied class strings), keeping `css_module` (the scoping bug + hashed classes for no gain). **BREAKING CHANGE**, by the row's own intent: `.dx-foo-<hash>` selectors must become `.dx-foo` for anyone selecting themed component classes externally | Landed 2026-09-04 | M (done) | Harness unaffected (all specs select by role/name). Verified by execution: 43/43 on the migration oracle; the delivered-but-not-obviously-wired `sidebar` case confirmed fixed; 86/86 on the CSS-sensitive specs (`select` including row 47's width cases, `sidebar`, `toast`, `touch-double-tap`, `global-stylesheet`); `cargo check`/`cargo fmt --check`/`cargo test -p preview` (6/6) clean; prefix, composition, cfg-axis and stylelint gates all exit 0. **2026-09-04 addendum:** row 47 is a second, independently-discovered instance of this exact scoping gap — this time in a *component's own* stylesheet (`select/style.css`'s `@supports (anchor-name: --a) { .dx-select-list[popover] { min-width: anchor-size(width); } }`, dead for the same reason the shared engine snippet's copies were) rather than a hand-copied engine snippet, confirming this is a bug class every themed `@supports` block is exposed to until this row's migration lands, not a one-off. A **third** instance surfaced 2026-09-04 in row 31a's design-token round: a first attempt to consolidate component `:focus-visible` rings into a new shared interaction layer by naming component classes directly in the global theme sheet hit the identical unhashed-selector-never-matches gap (confirmed against the built artifact) — not shipped; see row 31a for the full account. Fixed with `merge_attributes` (`5fc1439`, `preview/src/components/label/component.rs`), confirmed by Rule 4/4-extended now passing everywhere, not just the one field demo that surfaced it |
| 47 | **LANDED 2026-09-04: styled `Select` listbox rendered full viewport width on the home page gallery — the "dropdown menu has full page width" report, misidentified against `DropdownMenu` in the original (not-reproduced) write-up of this row.** Re-investigated against the actual subject: opening either styled `Select` on the home page (`/`, desktop 1280 wide, both the "Select an option" and "Choose a fruit" cards — also reproduces on `/component/?name=select&`) rendered `.dx-select-list-<hash>[popover]` at 1237–1247px wide against a 1280px viewport, clamped only by its own `max-width: calc(100vw - 2rem)`. Root cause: `min-width: 100%` in `preview/src/components/select/style.css` was written pre-migration, when this listbox was `position: absolute` inside `.dx-select`'s `position: relative` box (100% = trigger width); promoting it to the top layer via `popover` made its containing block the viewport instead, so the same percentage silently started meaning 100% of the *viewport*. A `@supports (anchor-name: --a) { .dx-select-list[popover] { min-width: anchor-size(width); } }` rule had already been added to restore trigger-relative sizing, but was dead in the served stylesheet: `#[css_module]`'s class-hashing does not scope selectors written only inside an `@supports` body (`dev-docs/issues/css-module-supports-scoping.md` — see row 32's notes for this being a second instance of the same gap), so the rule targeted the unhashed `.dx-select-list` while the live element carries `.dx-select-list-e8d9eb03`. And `use_anchor_position_fallback`'s JS fallback (the path a non-anchor engine like Firefox/WebKit takes) only ever corrected *position*, never *width* — so no engine had a working fix. Fixed at the engine level (`primitives/src/top_layer.rs`): every anchored content's trigger width is now published as the custom property `--dx-anchor-width`, unconditionally, from `use_anchor_position_fallback`'s own measurement (`reposition()`, every engine, kept fresh by its existing scroll/resize/visualViewport tracking) and, redundantly, from the shared `@supports` stylesheet's marker-class rules (`--dx-anchor-width: anchor-size(width);`, verified by execution to substitute correctly through a custom property on this sandbox's Chromium). `select/style.css` now reads `min-width: var(--dx-anchor-width, 100%)` and its dead `@supports` block/comment were rewritten to explain the contract. Regression oracle: two new `select.spec.ts` cases (component page and home gallery) asserting the listbox's width tracks its trigger and its left edge stays within 8px of the trigger's — RED before the fix (1237.76px / 1241.14px against ~141–156px triggers on a 1280px viewport, matching the report), GREEN after (listbox width equals trigger width exactly: 156.36px / 141.41px). `oracle/tier2-html/top-layer.spec.ts`'s own Rule 13 ("fit-content width") runs on that file's unstyled fixture and cannot see a themed component's CSS, so it never covered this — the new `select.spec.ts` cases are the real oracle for it; see that rule's own header doc, updated to say so | Landed | S–M | Every other themed overlay `style.css` (`dropdown_menu`, `menubar`, `navbar`, `context_menu`, `popover`, `tooltip`, `hover_card`, `date_picker`, `color_picker`) was checked for the same class of bug (a percentage `width`/`min-width` on a top-layer content element) and found clean — each uses a fixed pixel `min-width` (200px/220px) or a `max-width` clamp against the viewport that was already viewport-relative by design (`position: fixed`'s containing block is always the viewport, promoted or not). `combobox`'s list is a fixed `width: 200px` matching its input wrapper's own fixed `width: 200px` — no viewport-relative bug there, and no "wide input" case exists today to make matching via `--dx-anchor-width` worth doing; left as-is |

| 50 | **LANDED 2026-09-04: top-layer overlays inherited the UA's ink colour instead of the app's, sitewide.** User-reported against the deployed preview: Menubar submenu text and the Popover menu render the wrong colour. Root cause: `[popover]` and `<dialog>` each carry a UA stylesheet rule setting `color: CanvasText` DIRECTLY on the element, and a directly-cascaded value beats an inherited one regardless of specificity or source order — so every overlay wrapper that never declares its own `color` renders UA black instead of `--secondary-color-4`. Navbar only appeared correct because `.dx-navbar-item` declares its own colour and masks the identical bug; in dark mode the defect degrades to black-on-dark-grey. **Explicitly NOT a regression from rows 31b/32** — proven by building and serving the pre-session commit `963c570` side by side for byte-identical measurements, and by `git diff 963c570..HEAD` touching zero `color:` lines in those stylesheets; the hypothesis that row 32's per-component `document::Link` changed cascade order was falsified directly, since neither `main.css` nor `dx-components-theme.css` declares a `color` rule for these elements at all. Fixed as a class, not per instance, because row 44's `ensure_toast_base_styles` had already fixed exactly this for the raw toast and never generalised it: `ensure_top_layer_ink_styles` (`primitives/src/top_layer.rs`) injects zero-specificity `:where([popover]), :where(dialog) { color: inherit }` from the only three hooks by which anything enters the top layer — `use_popover_sync`, `use_popover_shown_while_mounted` and `use_dialog_open_driver` (`lib.rs`), covering the popover-promoted and native-`<dialog>` arms. Execution then showed the wrapper rule alone was insufficient: `<button>`/`<input>`/`<select>`/`<textarea>` carry their own direct UA colour, so DatePicker, Select and Combobox stayed black (their items are real buttons, e.g. `.dx-calendar-grid-cell`); the rule was extended to those controls **inside** a top-layer surface, scoped there rather than app-wide. Two instances the shared rule cannot fix alone were also fixed: `.dx-menubar-item` never declared a colour, and the Popover demo's `<h3>` was rendered with no class so `.dx-popover-content-title` never applied. Oracle: new `playwright/oracle/tier2-html/top-layer-ink.spec.ts`, 15 surfaces x light/dark, **red-first 20 failed/10 passed before, 30/30 after**, asserting against the token read from the page rather than a hardcoded rgb so it survives a theme retune, and deliberately retaining the cases where a component sets its own colour (tooltip; select/combobox's `--secondary-color-1`) as proof the shared rule does not override them. 199 further specs across the overlay family pass. **Known residue — CLOSED, same date.** The ink baseline was injected from its own `use_effect` while the overlay's `showPopover()`/`showModal()` ran from a different effect's `document::eval`, leaving a first-paint window (observed as the 2-in-N flake described above). Fixed with the construction this repo had already proven for the identical problem: `anchor_positioning_inject_js`'s own doc records that cross-`document::eval` ordering between two effects is unreliable even when both are declared, in order, in the same function, and that the fix is to prepend the injecting script directly into the dependent script so both run as one synchronous JS program with no ordering assumption left to make. `ensure_top_layer_ink_styles`'s injection is now prepended the identical way at all three top-layer entry hooks — `use_popover_sync`, `use_popover_shown_while_mounted` (`top_layer.rs`), and `use_dialog_open_driver` (`lib.rs`) — the same three exhaustive call sites the original fix touched. `toast.rs` was checked and deliberately left unchanged: it already shares this construction, reads nothing back that the window could race, and its own ink is covered via `use_popover_shown_while_mounted`. Proven by repetition rather than one run, since the defect was itself a flake: **150/150 across five repeats** of the full `top-layer-ink.spec.ts` oracle | Landed 2026-09-04 | S–M | Cross-reference the `css_module`/`@supports`-scoping bug class (rows 31a/32/47): this is a *different* bug mechanism (UA direct-cascade beating inheritance), not another instance of that one — noted so the two aren't conflated. See row 51 for a second, unrelated defect surfaced by this same investigation |
| 51 | **`Calendar`'s stylesheet does not load on the `date_picker` route — LANDED 2026-09-04.** Found incidentally during row 50's ink investigation: `document.styleSheets` on `/component/?name=date_picker&` contained no `calendar`-named entry at all, so `DatePicker`'s calendar day-grid cells (`.dx-calendar-grid-cell`, real `<button>`s) had no author `color` — or any other `Calendar` styling — to fall back to on that route, only their own UA default. Pre-existing and unrelated to row 50's fix (which no longer causes black text there regardless, since the extended rule reaches top-layer-descendant form controls independent of whether their own stylesheet loaded). **Root cause:** `date_picker` composes `CalendarRoot`/`CalendarMonthView` — the calendar primitive — rather than the themed `Calendar` wrapper, so after row 32 moved CSS delivery into each themed wrapper's own `document::Link`, `date_picker`'s composition path never rendered `Calendar`'s wrapper and never rendered its link either. **The audit matters more than the fix.** Checked as a class, not patched as a one-off bug: two shapes were considered — a raw `dioxus_primitives::` component composed outside its owning wrapper (already guarded by `check-preview-composition.sh`, zero violations across the codebase) and a themed component composing another's `pub(crate)` internals directly, which that script exempts wholesale rather than checking. Six components do the latter; of them, `date_picker`/`calendar` was the **only** one missing CSS. **Fixed** by moving the `document::Link` onto `CalendarRoot`/`RangeCalendarRoot` themselves — the `pub(crate)` roots every consumer (including `Calendar`/`RangeCalendar` themselves) funnels through — mirroring `popover`'s existing pattern of a `Link` on every one of its own composable entry points, not just its "main" one; `document::Link`'s `(href, rel)` dedup makes the now-redundant `Link` that used to sit only on `Calendar`/`RangeCalendar` harmless to leave, so it was simply removed. **The guard is generic, not a calendar special case:** `playwright/dx-class-migration.spec.ts` now asserts, per route, that every `dx-<component>` prefix actually rendered in that route's own demo content has its owning component's stylesheet reachable from the page — no per-component "what composes what" list needed, and it would have caught this pair red before the fix | Landed 2026-09-04 | S | Found 2026-09-04 building row 50's oracle; landed the same date rather than folded into row 50, since it is a separate, unrelated defect |

The module doc now lists Navbar among its consumers and names it in that carve-out, since listing only three consumers is how this gap went unnoticed in the first place. `playwright/oracle/tier1-apg/menu-roles.spec.ts` did **not** cover Navbar at all — the omission that let the drift happen unnoticed — and now does, opening by hover (Navbar's real gesture, not Menubar's click), with the same two-elements-both-`role="menu"` disambiguation the existing Menubar block already needed. See row 53 for the larger stage-2 question this stage was scoped out of |
| 53 | **Menu-family submenu consolidation, stage 2 — LANDED 2026-09-14, verified by execution.** Following stage 1 (row 52), the earlier assessment of whether `DropdownMenu`/`ContextMenu`/`Menubar` should share more plumbing concluded **extract three narrow bands, not a whole shared component** — that scope is what actually got built, in a new module `primitives/src/menu_root.rs` (registered via one `mod menu_root;` line in `lib.rs`), the same "identical plumbing here, host-private everything else" shape `primitives/src/menu_sub.rs` (row 54) already established as this crate's precedent. **The construction, band by band:** (1) `use_open_focus_sync` — the "open-index container": the effect that resyncs a menu's own `open` boolean from whether its roving-focus collection currently holds DOM focus (gated on no descendant submenu being open) was **byte-identical, word for word**, between `DropdownMenu`'s and `ContextMenu`'s root components before this change — now one shared function, called from both. **Deliberately not extended to `Menubar`**: that host's analogous effect resyncs a *which-index-is-open* signal (`Signal<Option<usize>>`), not a plain boolean, and has no `submenu_open_count` concept at all (`Menubar` doesn't support `DropdownMenuSub`-style nested submenus) — forcing the two shapes into one generic function would either lose the index `Menubar` needs or invent a translation layer with no existing duplication to justify it, so `Menubar`'s own version stays exactly where it was. (2) `use_menu_content_lifecycle` — the "content lifecycle/positioning pipeline": id resolution (`use_unique_id` + `use_id_or`, honoring a caller-overridden id) plus `use_animated_open`'s open/close-hold render gate, identical across all three hosts' `*Content` components — now one shared call in each, returning `(id, render)`. The *positioning* half of the name stays host-private, for the identical reason `menu_sub.rs`'s own doc gives for its own submenu content: `top_layer.rs` hard-codes a fixed list of `dx-anchor-*` marker classes, so the actual `use_anchor_position_fallback`/`use_point_anchor_clamp` call still has to live in each file's own `#[cfg(feature = "web")]` leaf render function — this band only factors the lifecycle gate that runs *before* that call. (3) `content_labelledby_attributes` — the "trigger id-plumbing": the APG-required accessible-name-from-trigger-id block (row 25) was duplicated across all six `*ContentRendered` web/native arm pairs (three hosts × two arms) — now one shared function called from all six. **What stayed host-private, exactly as the original assessment said it should:** the keyboard state machine (`MenubarMenu` still concentrates its whole contract in one handler; `Navbar` still splits it three ways) and the item components (`NavbarItem`'s routing-aware `Link` vs. `MenubarItem`'s plain `div`) — neither was touched, and `Navbar` itself was **not** made a consumer of `menu_root` at all, matching the original scoping to `DropdownMenu`/`ContextMenu`/`Menubar` only. `menu_sub.rs` was read for precedent and left untouched, per this row's own file-ownership rule — its nested-submenu state machine shares no code with this extraction (different level of nesting, already its own module). **The Escape-refocus divergence, investigated and root-caused, not left open:** `Menubar` explicitly refocuses its trigger on Escape; `Navbar`'s Escape handler did not. Root-caused as a **genuine oversight, not a deliberate hover-model exemption** — `Navbar` fully supports keyboard-driven opening (`NavbarNav`'s ArrowDown/ArrowUp move focus into the open content exactly the way `MenubarMenu`'s do), so a keyboard user who arrows into an open nav and presses Escape faces exactly the same "where does focus go" question Menubar already answers, and no test asserted either behavior before this fix. Fixed in `Navbar`'s own `onkeydown` (host-agnostic, not gated behind `#[cfg(feature = "web")]`, so it applies to both arms): read the open index before clearing it, then re-focus that trigger through the collection, mirroring `MenubarMenu`'s own "clear then set to force a real transition" construction exactly. **A genuine surprise found during verification, worth recording:** on this sandbox's Chromium, the new red-first test for this fix (see below) passed even with the fix *reverted* — root-caused, not just observed: `NavbarContent` (like `MenubarContent`) renders via `popover="auto"`, and the WHATWG Popover API's own "focus fixup" behavior already restores focus to the element that was focused before the popover opened whenever it closes (native light dismiss *or* a scripted `hidePopover()` call), which for `Navbar` happens to be the trigger itself in every case this bug could occur. So on the **web arm**, this specific defect was already invisible to Chromium's native popover behavior, independent of this fix. The fix is still real and still needed: it is not gated to the web arm (unlike `use_anchor_position_fallback`-style code), so it is the only thing providing this contract on the **native (Blitz) arm**, which has no Popover API and thus no such platform behavior — the same reason `MenubarMenu` doesn't rely on the platform either, and does the refocus explicitly itself. This could not be verified on Blitz in this sandbox (no such test harness exists here); the finding is disclosed rather than presented as a red→green proof on the engine actually exercised. **Red-first verification (per `dev-docs/plan.md`'s definition of done):** (a) For the three shared bands themselves: rather than inventing redundant coverage, existing specs already exercise all three hosts' open/close and positioning behavior end to end — `playwright/dropdown-menu.spec.ts`, `playwright/context-menu.spec.ts` (including its "touch tap outside closes the open menu" case, which exercises band 1's exact codepath), `playwright/menubar.spec.ts`, and the full `keyboard-matrix.spec.ts` open/close rows for all three hosts — all of which stayed green through the refactor, proving the extraction preserved behavior. The one genuinely new piece of coverage this row adds for bands 2/3 is the "trigger id-plumbing" band's `aria-labelledby` value, previously asserted only for `DropdownMenu` (`menu-roles.spec.ts`) — **new assertions added for `ContextMenu` and `Menubar`** (mirroring `DropdownMenu`'s existing one), which would catch a future drift between the three hosts' now-shared `content_labelledby_attributes` call. (b) For the Escape-refocus fix: a new `keyboard-matrix.spec.ts` case, `NavbarNav — Escape`, graded against `MenubarMenu`'s identical contract — confirmed it passes with the fix in place; per the finding above, it does not distinguish fixed-from-reverted on this sandbox's Chromium (both pass, for the platform-behavior reason described), so it is disclosed as a regression guard and a stated contract, not a red→green proof on this engine. **Verified by actual execution (Chromium, this sandbox), 2026-09-14:** `cargo check --workspace` and `cargo check --workspace --features web -p dioxus-primitives` both clean (only the two pre-existing, unrelated `preview` warnings present on `main`); `cargo fmt --check` clean; `cargo clippy --workspace --tests --examples -- -D warnings` fails exactly as this task's own brief predicted — 5 pre-existing `doc_lazy_continuation` errors in `menu_sub.rs` (a file this row did not touch), and **zero** new warnings or errors anywhere else, confirmed by grepping the full clippy output for every touched file. `npx playwright test --project=chromium oracle/tier1-apg/keyboard-matrix.spec.ts oracle/tier1-apg/menu-roles.spec.ts dropdown-menu.spec.ts context-menu.spec.ts menubar.spec.ts navbar.spec.ts` (97 tests, Chromium only, `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH=/opt/pw-browsers` already matching this repo's pinned Playwright 1.60.0/chromium-1223): **96 passed, 1 skipped (a pre-existing, unrelated `test.skip` in the Accordion section), 0 failed.** Firefox/WebKit not attempted (disallowed in this sandbox, per row 4) | Landed 2026-09-14 | M (done) | Files touched: `primitives/src/menu_root.rs` (new), `primitives/src/lib.rs` (+1 line), `primitives/src/dropdown_menu.rs`, `primitives/src/context_menu.rs`, `primitives/src/menubar.rs` (all three: swap the duplicated blocks for the shared calls, no behavior change), `primitives/src/navbar.rs` (the Escape-refocus fix only), `playwright/oracle/tier1-apg/keyboard-matrix.spec.ts` (+Navbar Escape case), `playwright/oracle/tier1-apg/menu-roles.spec.ts` (+ContextMenu/Menubar labelledby assertions). Builds on the top-layer/popover engine (Phase 4.4) and `menu_semantics` (row 52), same as row 54 |
| 54 | **Nested submenus (`DropdownMenu.Sub`/`ContextMenu.Sub`) — LANDED 2026-09-04, verified by execution.** Closes the "Nested submenu" row in [`component-backlog.md`](./component-backlog.md) (rated **L**, called "the highest-value primitive-needing gap with no clean source") — see that file's own row for the catalog-status update. Built on the landed top-layer/popover engine (Phase 4.4, row 2) and reading its roles from `menu_semantics` (row 24/52), so it inherits both foundations rather than re-deriving them. New shared module `primitives/src/menu_sub.rs` factors the plumbing genuinely identical between `DropdownMenu`'s and `ContextMenu`'s submenus — open/close state, the submenu's own roving-focus item collection, the trigger/content id pair, and the hover-intent open/close timer (`DelayedAction`, generalised from `context_menu.rs`'s pre-existing long-press timer, this crate's only prior art for a timed pointer-driven open/close) — while deliberately leaving each host's own private root context lookup and content-positioning call in `dropdown_menu.rs`/`context_menu.rs` themselves, since Rust's module privacy has no way to let a shared module see either host's private context type without widening its API surface for no reader benefit. Scoped to one level of nesting only, matching the module's own doc and this row's original **L** rating: a `Sub`'s own content may hold plain items but not a further nested `Sub`, since a sub-trigger registers in the *root* menu's collection by reading that root's context type directly, which only resolves correctly at exactly one level of depth. New APG oracle `playwright/oracle/tier1-apg/menu-submenu.spec.ts` (430 lines, cited to the same pinned APG "Menu and Menubar" pattern commit `keyboard-matrix.spec.ts` already uses) — passing. A `use_effect_cleanup` fix was needed in both `dropdown_menu.rs` and `context_menu.rs` to make submenus actually open at all: a submenu's own open-count bookkeeping effect had no cleanup path for the case of the enclosing menu (or an ancestor submenu) unmounting the submenu while it was still open, which runs no further effects, only cleanups — without it the shared open-count state could desync and submenus would fail to open. Keyboard-matrix stayed **64/64** with no regressions | Landed 2026-09-04 | L | References checked, per `lifting-from-forks.md` hygiene, and NOT taken: `molikto/dioxus-components@ed681439` (stale, shape-only, pre-dates the collection/focus refactors this repo now has) |
| 55 | **`Sidebar` right-side mirroring silently no-op'd when `collapsible: SidebarCollapsible::None` — LANDED 2026-09-15, user-reported.** `Sidebar()`'s `None` branch renders a parallel `.dx-sidebar-static` element (distinct from the main desktop branch's `.dx-sidebar-container`), and unlike that branch it never set `data-side`. The only CSS rule mirroring the sidebar to the right (`style.css`: `.dx-sidebar-wrapper:has([data-side="right"]) { flex-direction: row-reverse; }`, previously scoped to `.dx-sidebar-desktop[data-side="right"]`) requires that attribute, so with "Collapse: None" selected in the demo's own settings panel, toggling "Side: Right" had zero visual effect — the sidebar stayed pinned left. The default Offcanvas/Icon collapse modes already mirrored correctly; the bug was scoped specifically to `None`, which is why it only appeared "when choosing other configs." A second, same-root-cause defect found alongside it: `.dx-sidebar-static` also never set any `position`, so its child `SidebarRail` (`position: absolute`) had no positioned ancestor and rendered pinned to a page corner instead of the sidebar's own edge, for both sides. **Fixed by construction, not per-instance:** (1) `component.rs` — added `data-side` to the `None` branch's attributes; (2) `style.css` — broadened the mirroring selector to key on `[data-slot="sidebar"][data-side="right"]` (a slot all three Sidebar render paths already share) instead of the desktop-only class, so one selector covers every current and future branch; added `position: relative` to `.dx-sidebar-static`, giving `SidebarRail` the same containing block the main branch already gets for free from `.dx-sidebar-container`'s `position: fixed`. **Verified by execution:** `getBoundingClientRect()`/`getComputedStyle()` before/after at 1280x800 — sidebar rect identical for Left/Right before the fix (bug), correctly mirrored after (`x=0–256` vs `x=1009–1265`); `SidebarRail` moved from a page-corner rect (`x=1257–1273` / `x=-8–8`) to hugging the sidebar edge (`x=248–264` / `x=1001–1017`) on both sides. New geometry-based regression test `playwright/sidebar.spec.ts`, "desktop: side switch mirrors sidebar with collapsible=none" (mirrors `select.spec.ts`'s convention — asserts rects actually swap, not just that `data-side` changes, since the pre-existing "side switch updates data-side" test only checked the attribute and is exactly why it missed this). Full `sidebar.spec.ts` suite: **7/7 passed** (6 pre-existing + 1 new), no regressions. The test's own width assertion was initially written against `document.documentElement.clientWidth` and failed by exactly 15px — root-caused to row 1's own `scrollbar-gutter: stable` baseline (not a new bug), fixed by comparing against the wrapper element's own `boundingClientRect` instead | Landed 2026-09-15 | S | Found via user report ("alignment bugs" when "choosing other configs, like the right side"); delegated to a Sonnet subagent per this repo's debugging convention, independently re-verified (including the scrollbar-gutter false start) before landing |

| 56 | **Docs site now dogfoods the real `Sidebar` component as its own navigation — LANDED 2026-09-15, user-requested.** The docs site's own nav was a hand-rolled `DocsSidebar` in `main.rs` (raw `<aside>`/`<nav>`/`<button>` elements, its own `dx-docs-sidebar-*` classes, its own toggle/backdrop) — a parallel implementation duplicating what the shipped `Sidebar`/`SidebarProvider`/`SidebarInset` already do, never dogfooded even on the site showcasing them. Replaced with a new `DocsLayout` composing `SidebarProvider`/`Sidebar`/`SidebarContent`/`SidebarInset`, mirroring the composition `sidebar/variants/main/mod.rs`'s own demo and the email-client dashboard already use (`SidebarMenuButton`'s `as` prop swaps in the real router `Link` for SPA navigation; `SidebarInset` supplies the page's one `<main>` landmark). Applied to all three site pages — `/docs`, each component page, and (newly) the **homepage** `/` itself, whose hero + component gallery previously had no sidebar nav at all and are now the `SidebarInset` content of a real sidebar layout. `DocsNavActive` replaces the old `Option<&'static str>` active-param, which conflated "on Docs" with "on Home" (both were `None`) — each page now gets its own distinct active state, verified via direct DOM query (not just visual) to confirm zero false-positive active links on the homepage. **A real cross-cutting CSS conflict found and fixed along the way:** `sidebar/style.css`'s desktop rendering assumes its wrapper IS the whole page (`position: fixed` column, `100svh` tall) — true for the two other call sites (`ComponentBlockDemo`, `EmailClientDashboard`), both rendered outside the site's own Navbar/Footer chrome; `DocsLayout` shares the page with those, so a fixed column would float over the footer past the fold. Re-scoped under `.dx-sidebar-wrapper.dx-docs-shell` to a `position: sticky` in-flow column with a width-collapse instead of the fixed-position slide-off, keeping the same collapse transition without fighting page chrome the component doesn't own. **Verified by execution:** `cargo check`/`cargo fmt --check` clean; `check-preview-composition.sh`/`check-dx-class-prefix.sh`/`check-css-literals.sh` all green; `preview.spec.ts` (axe on home + a component page), `sidebar.spec.ts`, `navbar.spec.ts` — 13/13 passed, independently re-run; active-nav-link state and footer/sidebar non-overlap after scrolling confirmed via direct DOM query and screenshot, not visual inspection alone | Landed 2026-09-15 | M | User-requested after two rejected placement options (a homepage-grid card, a small thumbnail) clarified the actual ask was site-wide nav dogfooding, not a demo-card change; delegated to a Sonnet subagent, independently re-verified before landing. `oracle/hydration-parity.spec.ts` not run — needs a separate SSG build lane orthogonal to this composition-only change |
| 57 | **`ContextMenu`/`DropdownMenu` submenus closed the instant the pointer left the trigger toward the submenu's own content — LANDED 2026-09-15, user-reported.** The pre-existing hover oracle (`menu-submenu.spec.ts`) stayed green because its one hover test only ever moved the pointer from the trigger to a *sibling root item*, never into the submenu's own content — exactly the path the report described ("on sub-menu mouse hover, the sub-menu disappears / submenus not selectable"). **Root cause 1 (shared plumbing, both hosts):** `SUBMENU_CLOSE_GRACE_DELAY`'s `DelayedAction` timer was private local state inside `*SubTrigger` only (`menu_sub.rs`); the trigger's own `onmouseleave` scheduled a close on it, but **`*SubContentRendered` had no `onmouseenter`/`onmouseleave` handlers at all**, so nothing could ever cancel that timer once the pointer entered the submenu's own content — ~200ms later the stale close fired regardless of where the pointer actually was. Fixed by construction: hoisted `hover_open`/`hover_close: DelayedAction` onto the shared `SubMenuState` (read via context by both trigger and content instead of the trigger's own private locals), then wired `onmouseenter`/`onmouseleave` on all four `*SubContentRendered` functions (`ContextMenuSubContentRendered`/`DropdownMenuSubContentRendered`, web + native arms) to cancel/reschedule the same shared timer the trigger already does — this alone fully fixed `DropdownMenu.Sub`. **Root cause 2 (`ContextMenu`-specific, on top of root cause 1):** `ContextMenu.Sub` still failed after the above — `.dx-context-menu-content` (shared by `ContextMenuContent` and `ContextMenuSubContent`) sets `pointer-events: none` **unconditionally**, unlike `.dx-dropdown-menu-content`'s identical-looking reset, correctly scoped to `[data-state="closed"]` only. `ContextMenuContentRendered` (the root) already works around this with an inline `pointer_events: open().then_some("auto")` override, but `ContextMenuSubContentRendered` never had the equivalent — so an open submenu was non-interactive the entire time, explaining why root cause 1's new hover handlers couldn't even fire. Fixed by mirroring the same inline override onto both of `ContextMenuSubContentRendered`'s arms. `DropdownMenu` never needed this fix (confirmed via grep — no `pointer_events` usage in `dropdown_menu.rs` at all, since its CSS already scopes the reset correctly). **Verified by execution:** two new regression tests in `menu-submenu.spec.ts` (real `page.mouse.move(..., {steps})` sequences, not `.hover()`, from trigger onto the submenu's own item, past the grace delay) — full oracle **14/14 passed** (5 pre-existing unchanged + 2 new, both hosts); `context-menu.spec.ts` + `dropdown-menu.spec.ts` + `keyboard-matrix.spec.ts` combined, independently re-run: **82 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failed**; `cargo check` clean for both default and `--features web` on `primitives`, `cargo fmt --check` clean, all three composition/class-prefix/css-literal lint scripts green | Landed 2026-09-15 | M | Found via user report; delegated to a Sonnet subagent, independently re-verified (including a full oracle + broad regression re-run, not just the two new tests) before landing |

| 58 | **`Sidebar` block-demo iframe rendered empty on live production; homepage Sidebar card now exposes live config for the real site nav — LANDED 2026-09-15, user-reported + user-requested.** Visiting `/component/?name=sidebar&` on the deployed site showed the demo's own Side/Collapse settings panel rendering and responding to clicks, but the actual `Sidebar` preview never appeared — no `[data-slot="sidebar"]` node anywhere, just empty space, on every collapse/side combination. **Root cause:** `Sidebar`'s own responsive check (`component.rs`, `MOBILE_BREAKPOINT = 768`, `use_is_mobile()`) reads `window.innerWidth` of whatever document it's running in — for an `<iframe>`, that's the iframe's own content window, not the outer page's viewport. The block demo's iframe (`BlockComponentVariantHighlight`) was `width: 100%` inside the page's content column; once row 56 (above) narrowed that column with the new site-nav sidebar, the iframe's measured `innerWidth` dropped to ~684px — under the 768px breakpoint — flipping `Sidebar` into its mobile branch, which renders a closed `Sheet` (nothing painted) rather than the desktop branch. Proven directly on the live, unmodified production site: widening the existing iframe in place from 684px→820px via the exact `resize`-event path `use_is_mobile()` listens for flipped the rendered sidebar count 0→1. **Fixed** by giving the shared iframe a `min-width: 820px` (comfortably clear of the breakpoint) plus `overflow-x: auto` on its wrapper (`html,body{overflow-x:clip}` in `main.css` would otherwise silently clip rather than scroll) — one shared construction point (`BlockComponentVariantHighlight`), so it covers every current and future `ComponentType::Block` component's iframe, not just Sidebar's. **Second part (user-requested):** the homepage's Sidebar gallery card previously just linked out ("Open full preview") instead of showing anything live. `DocsLayout` now takes optional `side`/`collapsible` signals (every other call site still gets its own fixed local defaults, seeded identically to before, so no behavior change there); `Home()` owns and provides them via a `HomeSidebarControls` context; `ComponentGalleryPreview`'s `Block` branch, only for `component.name == "sidebar"` (`try_consume_context`, degrading gracefully to the old link anywhere else), swaps the link for `SidebarGalleryCardControls` — the same Side/Collapse button shape as the demo's own `DemoSettingControls`, but writing straight to those signals, so clicking them visibly reconfigures the real, on-page site sidebar live. Confirmed no state leakage across navigation (Dioxus remounts `DocsLayout` fresh per route). **A second, unrelated-but-newly-exposed layout bug found while verifying this:** at a plain 1280px viewport the new card's Side/Collapse controls rendered genuinely off-screen and unclickable (not a scroll issue — `document.documentElement.scrollWidth` was *less* than `clientWidth`, so the content was clipped, not scrollable-to). Root-caused to a classic Flexbox pitfall, not this card's own layout: `.dx-sidebar-inset` is `flex: 1 1 0%` with no `min-width` override, so its *automatic* minimum width defaults to its content's min-content size rather than 0 (CSS Flexbox spec) — any wide-enough content anywhere inside the inset (here, the pre-existing, unrelated `WidgetMasonry` homepage widget, confirmed by live DOM bisection removing subtrees one at a time) makes the whole inset refuse to shrink and silently overflow (`overflow-x: clip` on `html`/`body` hides it rather than scrolling), which in turn widens every sibling section sharing that inset's `width: 100%` — the gallery card whose own content happened to reach flush to its card's now-off-screen edge was just the first thing to make it visible and unclickable. **Fixed by construction, at the one flex item that needs to shrink** (`min-width: 0` on `.dx-sidebar-inset`) rather than re-constraining whichever content exposes it — covers `WidgetMasonry` today and any future wide content by the same mechanism, per this repo's standing "class not instance" rule. **A pre-existing, unrelated `dx build --ssg` tooling race found and guarded against during verification:** an independently-reproduced, systematic 9-build A/B test (alternating branch/baseline source, full `rm -rf target/dx/preview` each time) showed **100% correlation between build speed and correctness, 0% correlation with source content** — every build that had to actually recompile the `preview` crate (cold cache) came out correct on both branch and pristine baseline; every build that hit a warm cache (~10s, skip straight to bundling/SSG) came out with every non-home SSG route missing its `<script type="module">` hydration bootstrap tag entirely on both branch and baseline. This is a `dx`-cli/dioxus-fullstack race (the SSG render pass fires all routes' requests at the freshly-started fullstack server concurrently, seemingly before some server-side dependency of the client-bundling phase is ready) — not caused by, or fixable from, this app's own code. Guarded against rather than left latent: `scripts/deploy-preview.sh` now greps every built route's `index.html` for the bootstrap script tag right after the build and exits 1 with the affected route list before touching `/docs` if any are missing — verified both halves for real (a genuine cold `cargo clean` + full rebuild passes silently; re-running immediately after, cache now warm, naturally hit the same race and the script correctly caught it and exited 1 without touching `docs/`). **Verified by execution, independently re-checked twice more by the orchestrating session after two rounds of subagent-reported-but-unverified issues** (the SSG race initially mis-attributed to the diff; the card-overflow bug initially mis-attributed to the card's own layout) — both corrected via direct measurement before landing: `cargo check --workspace`/`cargo fmt --check` clean; `check-preview-composition.sh`/`check-dx-class-prefix.sh`/`check-css-literals.sh` clean; final clean `deploy-preview.sh` run passed its own new sanity check (6/6 routes); iframe `innerWidth: 820-822`, sidebar count 1, for all three Sidebar demo variants; homepage card's `Right`/`None` buttons clickable without `force`, `data-side` flips `left`→`right` live, `/docs` still defaults `left` after navigating away (no leakage); `document.documentElement.scrollWidth <= clientWidth` at 1280px | Landed 2026-09-15 | M | Both reports from direct user testing of the live deployed site; delegated to a Sonnet subagent across three rounds (bug1+feature2 → SSG-race investigation → card-overflow fix), each round independently re-verified against a real browser and a real `deploy-preview.sh` run before accepting, not just the subagent's own claims |

| 59 | **Block-demo iframe's row-58 `min-width: 820px` fix itself broke real mobile viewports; homepage nav now closed by default; homepage checkbox+label gap fixed to match convention — LANDED 2026-09-17, user-reported (×3) + user-requested (×1).** Row 58's flat, always-on `min-width: 820px` on `BlockComponentVariantHighlight`'s iframe fixed the desktop-squeeze case but fired unconditionally, so a genuinely narrow real device/window (390px) also got forced to an 822px-wide iframe — confirmed live on production (`getBoundingClientRect()`: iframe `width: 822` inside a 390px viewport, clipped rather than scrolled since `overflow-x: clip` on `html`/`body` hides the escape). **Fixed by construction:** moved the rule out of the iframe's own inline style/document into a new `.dx-block-demo-iframe` class in `preview/assets/main.css` — a stylesheet loaded by the OUTER top-level page, not the iframe's — gated behind `@media (min-width: 768px)` there. A media query written inside the iframe's own document can only ever see the iframe's own (possibly-squeezed) width, the exact ambiguity at the root of both this bug and row 58's original one; evaluating the query in the outer page's stylesheet instead reads the real visitor viewport, correctly telling apart "outer page's own nav squeezed an otherwise-wide desktop viewport" (>=768px outer: still force 820px, reachable via the wrapper's existing `overflow-x: auto`) from "this is a genuinely narrow device" (<768px outer: no forced min-width, iframe sizes to its real container, embedded `Sidebar`'s own `MOBILE_BREAKPOINT` check sees the true narrow width and renders its real mobile Sheet-trigger layout). One shared construction point, so it covers every current and future `ComponentType::Block` iframe, not just Sidebar's (currently the only one). **Second report, diagnosed as a false positive, not a real bug:** "homepage sidebar config does not work" — the homepage card's Side/Collapse buttons write straight to the same signals `DocsLayout` passes into the real `Sidebar`, confirmed still correct; on mobile the `Sidebar`'s Sheet branch simply isn't mounted until opened, so a card click has nothing visible to update and reasonably read as broken. No code bug found here; see the next item for what mobile actually needed. (One true incidental finding, not itself a bug: the mobile Sheet branch never reads the `collapsible` prop at all, so Offcanvas/Icon/None render identically on mobile — pre-existing, correct, unchanged.) **Third, user-requested: hide the homepage's own site nav by default.** `SidebarProvider`'s existing `default_open: bool` prop (previously unused by `DocsLayout`) is now threaded through a new `DocsLayout { default_open: Option<bool> }` prop; every call site but `Home()` passes `None` and keeps the previous open-by-default behavior, `Home()` alone passes `Some(false)` — the homepage's hero + gallery get full width on first load, the topbar `SidebarTrigger` still opens it identically to before. **Fourth, user-requested: checkbox+label gap.** The homepage's `BlockFilters` dashboard-mockup tag-checkbox row used an inline, inconsistent `gap: 0.55rem` (8.8px) instead of this codebase's established checkbox/radio-to-label spacing token, `var(--dx-space-3)` (12px, already used consistently by `Field`'s horizontal orientation and `RadioGroupItem`) — the other, unrelated `checkbox`/`field` component stylesheets were audited and found already correct, so this was the one actual instance.
