| 60 | **Input OTP: almost no hit target for the input — LANDED 2026-09-17, user-reported.** Not a geometry/sizing bug, despite the report's own framing: the real, invisible (`opacity: 0`) `<input>` in `primitives/src/input_otp.rs` was already correctly sized to match its full visual row (confirmed unchanged: `position: absolute; inset: 0; width/height: 100%`). The actual cause was paint order — a purely decorative, `aria-hidden` overlay `div` (the slot/separator visuals, rendered *after* the real input in the same stacking context) covers almost the entire row and, with the browser default `pointer-events: auto`, silently intercepts clicks before they reach the input beneath it. Confirmed directly via `elementFromPoint`: clicking dead-center of a visible slot resolved to that slot's own decorative `<div>`, not `#otp-main`; only the few-pixel gaps *between* slots (touched by no decorative box) ever reached the real input — exactly matching the report's "almost no hit target." **Fixed** with a one-line `pointer_events: "none"` on the overlay wrapper `div`, inherited by every descendant (no child sets its own `pointer-events`), making the whole decorative layer transparent to hit-testing to match how it's already transparent visually. New regression coverage in `playwright/input_otp.spec.ts`: a full-row bounding-box geometry check, and a real hit-test clicking all 6 slots' centers *and* corners, confirming every point now focuses the real input. **Verified by execution, independently re-checked by the orchestrating session:** `cargo check --workspace`/`cargo fmt --check` clean; all three composition/class-prefix/css-literal lint scripts clean; full `input_otp.spec.ts` suite re-run directly against a live `dx serve` instance — **7/7 passed on Chromium** (5 pre-existing unchanged + 2 new), independently confirming the subagent's own claimed pass (not just trusting its report) | Landed 2026-09-17 | S | User-reported; delegated to a Sonnet subagent alongside row 59's sidebar-cluster lane and a checkbox-padding audit (disjoint files: `primitives/src/input_otp.rs` + `playwright/input_otp.spec.ts` only), independently re-verified — cargo/lint/Playwright, no shortcuts taken on the subagent's claims — before landing |
| 61 | **Sidebar toggle icon lived in its own strip below the main site navbar instead of inside it — LANDED 2026-09-17, user-reported.** `Navbar` (`main.rs`) was rendered in `NavigationLayout` as a sibling of, and *before*, `Outlet::<Route> {}` — never a descendant of `DocsLayout`'s `SidebarProvider`, which is what actually owns the `SidebarCtx` a `SidebarTrigger` needs (`use_sidebar()` panics without an ancestor provider). `DocsLayout` compensated by rendering its own separate `header { class: "dx-docs-inset-topbar", SidebarTrigger {} }` strip below the navbar — the bug. **Fixed by construction:** made `Navbar` an actual descendant of the `SidebarProvider` on every route that has one, via one defensive check (`try_consume_context::<SidebarCtx>().is_some()`) instead of two separate code paths — this single check subsumes the desktop nav row, the `in_iframe` chromeless nav row, `Demos` (no sidebar at all), and `ComponentDemo`'s "component not found" early-return branch (also bypasses `DocsLayout`, previously lost its navbar entirely once `NavigationLayout` stopped rendering one unconditionally — now gets the same trigger-less `Navbar {}` as `Demos`). **The CSS trap this ran into:** `SidebarProvider`'s own render is just `div { class: "dx-sidebar-wrapper", {children} }`, and the base `.dx-sidebar-wrapper` rule (`sidebar/style.css`) is `display: flex` — a ROW sized for exactly two children, `Sidebar` + `SidebarInset`. Dropping `Navbar {}` in as a third child of that same row rendered it as a third flex item sitting beside them, not a full-width row stacked above. Fixed by grouping `Sidebar`+`SidebarInset` under one new wrapper, `.dx-docs-shell-body` (`main.css`), and making `.dx-sidebar-wrapper.dx-docs-shell` itself a `flex-direction: column` of exactly two children: `Navbar`, then that body row — the former outer-wrapper geometry (width/border/centering/the sidebar-row min-height) moved down onto `.dx-docs-shell-body`, since it's now the box that must match the navbar's own centered, content-width inner row. The base `@media (width >= 768px) { .dx-sidebar-wrapper:has([data-side="right"]) { flex-direction: row-reverse } }` rule (keyed on `:has()`, so it matches ANY `.dx-sidebar-wrapper` containing a right-sided sidebar anywhere inside it) would otherwise have reversed `Navbar` and the body row too, now that they share the same outer wrapper — cancelled at that level and reapplied one level down on `.dx-docs-shell-body`, where it correctly mirrors just the `Sidebar`/`SidebarInset` pair without moving `Navbar`. Neither of the *other* two `.dx-sidebar-wrapper` call sites (the block-demo iframe, the email-client dashboard) were touched — their selectors were left exactly as-is, only the docs-shell-specific ones were rescoped. **Verified by execution, independently re-checked by the orchestrating session, including tracking down a false alarm before landing:** `cargo check --workspace`/`cargo fmt --check` clean; `check-preview-composition.sh`/`check-dx-class-prefix.sh`/`check-css-literals.sh` all clean; `sidebar.spec.ts`/`navbar.spec.ts`/`preview.spec.ts`/`oracle/tier2-html/top-layer.spec.ts`/`oracle/tier3-radix/scroll-lock.spec.ts` re-run — the two `top-layer.spec.ts` "flip above bottom-edge trigger" failures reproduced identically against the unmodified baseline (`git stash`), confirming them pre-existing and unrelated (the real docs sidebar, dogfooded onto the `top_layer` oracle fixture page by row 56, intercepting a hover at that specific fixture layout — not caused by this change); a `navbar.spec.ts` "keyboard navigation" failure seen only when all five specs ran together turned out to be parallel-worker contention, not a real regression (passed cleanly alone and in smaller batches). **The false alarm, disclosed rather than silently discarded:** independently measuring the homepage's Sidebar gallery card after this fix initially showed its "Right"/"Left" controls rendered ~1250px off-screen and `.dx-sidebar-wrapper.dx-docs-shell`'s computed `flex-direction` reading `row` instead of `column` — looked exactly like a real regression (the same class of Flexbox/masonry-interaction bug row 58 already fixed once). Root-caused instead to this session's own `dx serve` dev-instance serving a stale cached CSS bundle that was missing the new rule entirely, confirmed by rebuilding via the production `dx build --ssg` recipe (`scripts/deploy-preview.sh`) and re-measuring against that: `flex-direction: column`, card at `x: 77.5` (matches baseline's `x: 76.5`), `Right` click correctly flips `data-side`, no console errors — this class of dev-server staleness is a known, already-documented `dx` tooling quirk (row 58's SSG cache-warm race is the closest precedent), not a new construction to add here | Landed 2026-09-17 | M | User-reported ("the sidebar icon in the overview should be in the main top bar"); delegated to a single Sonnet subagent (files: `main.rs`, `main.css` only), independently re-verified — cargo/lint, the full regression suite (with the two pre-existing top-layer flakes confirmed via baseline stash-diff, not just accepted on the subagent's word), and fresh production-build measurements after tracking down and ruling out a dev-server-caching false alarm — before landing |
| 62 | **`/docs` sidebar's "Start" group had a single "Overview" link and no icons anywhere in the site nav — LANDED 2026-09-17, user-requested.** The user asked for the Overview page's sidebar to expose its own real content (jump-to-section links, generated from its actual headings, not hand-typed) plus icons across the sidebar. **Generated, not duplicated:** a single `const DOCS_SECTIONS: &[&str]` (`main.rs`) and one `docs_section_slug` helper (lowercases, collapses non-alphanumeric runs to one hyphen) are the one source of truth for the `/docs` page's three `h2` headings' visible text, their `id`s, and the sidebar sub-items' labels/`href`s — all four render sites read from the same array/fn, so they cannot drift out of sync with each other the way two hand-typed copies of the same string eventually would. **Wired through `DocsLayout`** via a new `#[props(default)] docs_sections: Option<&'static [&'static str]>`, following the exact `side`/`collapsible`/`default_open` pattern already established there — only `Docs()` passes `Some(DOCS_SECTIONS)`; `Home()`/`ComponentHighlight()` pass nothing and render the "Start" group exactly as before (verified structurally unchanged on both). **Nesting** reuses the existing `SidebarMenuSub`/`SidebarMenuSubItem`/`SidebarMenuSubButton` primitives (`sidebar/component.rs`) — the same ones `sidebar/variants/*/mod.rs`'s own demos already use for nested sub-lists — rather than inventing new markup. **"Jump there" is a plain `a { href: "#slug" }`** via the `as` escape hatch, not the router's `Link`, since this is a same-page anchor scroll, not a route change; `html { scroll-behavior: smooth }` was added (`main.css`, alongside the pre-existing `scroll-padding-top` rule for the sticky navbar) so the jump animates instead of cutting. **Icons** (all `dioxus_icons::lucide`, already imported elsewhere in this file): `BookOpen` on "Start", `FileText` on "Overview", `Hash` (the conventional anchor/TOC marker) on each heading-derived sub-item, and one distinct icon per `ComponentCategory` group (Forms → `SquareCheck`, Navigation → `Compass`, Overlays → `Layers`, Feedback → `Bell`, Disclosure → `ChevronsUpDown`, Data display → `LayoutGrid`) — every icon carries `aria-hidden="true"` (mirroring the dashboard's own `LucideIcon` convention) since each sits beside a visible text label, so axe has no accessible-name gap to flag. Deliberately out of scope: per-individual-component icons (30+ distinct components, a much larger and more subjective task) — only category-level and the new section links got one. `SidebarGroupLabel` gained a `gap` in its CSS (`sidebar/style.css`) to support this icon+label pairing — it had none, being text-only until now; `SidebarMenuButton`/`SidebarMenuSubButton` already had the right icon+label spacing from existing collapsed-icon-mode CSS, so nothing else was needed. **Verified by execution, independently re-checked by the orchestrating session (concurrently with row 63's unrelated Input OTP fix, disjoint files, both re-verified separately before landing):** `cargo check --workspace`/`cargo fmt --check` clean; `check-preview-composition.sh`/`check-dx-class-prefix.sh`/`check-css-literals.sh` all clean; `sidebar.spec.ts`/`navbar.spec.ts`/`preview.spec.ts` re-run, 13/13 passed; a fresh manual axe scan against `/docs` itself (not covered by any pre-existing spec) came back with **0 violations**; independently confirmed against a live `dx serve` instance: exactly 3 sub-items under "Start" with the right labels and one icon each, clicking "Add a component" moves that `h2` from `y: 998` to `y: 144` (a real scroll, not just a URL change, and the URL stays on `/docs`), `/` and a component page both show 0 sub-items under "Overview" (unaffected) | Landed 2026-09-17 | M | User-requested; delegated to a single Sonnet subagent (files: `main.rs`, `sidebar/style.css`, `main.css` only), independently re-verified — cargo/lint, the existing suite, and a fresh manual axe scan plus live scroll-behavior measurement not covered by any pre-existing test — before landing |
| 63 | **Input OTP click-to-caret positioning did not generalize past the first slot — LANDED 2026-09-17, user-reported.** Row 60 fixed the OTP input's hit-target (every slot's box now correctly hands a click through to the real, invisible `<input>` via `pointer-events: none` on the decorative overlay) but left a second, deeper bug the user then caught: clicking slot 0 felt normal, but no other slot did. Root-caused live, before writing any fix: on an **empty** field, clicking slot 0, 3, or 5 all placed the caret at `selectionStart` 0; on a field filled with `"123"`, clicking slot 0 landed at index 2 while slots 1/4/5 all landed at index 3. Cause: the real `<input>` is `opacity: 0` with `font-family: inherit` and no `letter-spacing` tuned to the visual 40px slot grid (`input_otp/style.css`'s `.dx-input-otp-input` sets none) — a native browser places a click's caret by mapping its x-coordinate to where it thinks the *actual rendered glyphs* are, and since those invisible glyphs were never spatially aligned to the visible boxes, that mapping is essentially arbitrary past the trivial "empty field, nothing to click past" case that made slot 0 look uniquely correct. This is exactly why upstream `input-otp` (the library shadcn/ui's own `InputOTP` wraps) never trusts native click-to-glyph placement at all — it computes the clicked slot from geometry and calls `setSelectionRange` explicitly, which is what this fix does too. **Fixed** with a new `onclick` handler on the real input (`primitives/src/input_otp.rs`) that reads the click's client coordinates and spawns a one-shot `document::eval` (`snap_caret_to_slot`, mirroring this same file's own `use_caret_sync` and `virtual_list.rs`'s `sync_container_scroll` for the "one-shot eval, not a persistent listener" shape) that does a plain point-in-rect test against every `[data-slot-index]` element's own `getBoundingClientRect()` — not `elementFromPoint`, which the row-60 `pointer-events: none` fix makes blind to the slots entirely — scoped to this specific `InputOtp` instance's own overlay sibling so more than one on a page stays correct, and needing no special-casing for `InputOtpGroup`/`InputOtpSeparator` grouping since every slot's own rect is tested regardless of which group it's in. **Clamping rule:** `min(clicked_slot_index, value.chars().count())` — not an arbitrary choice, the only one `HTMLInputElement.setSelectionRange` itself can honor, since it clamps its own arguments to `[0, value.length]`. Consequence, confirmed and treated as correct rather than a shortfall: on a genuinely empty value every slot's click still lands at index 0, because no real `<input>` can place a caret past text that doesn't exist yet — verified this doesn't make the UI lie about it (`data-active` stays `true` only on slot 0, `false` on every other clicked-but-necessarily-clamped slot). **Verified by execution, independently re-checked by the orchestrating session:** `cargo check --workspace`/`cargo fmt --check` clean; all three composition/class-prefix/css-literal lint scripts clean; before/after comparison via a genuine `git stash`/unstash of just this file (not hypothetical) reproduced the exact bug numbers above pre-fix and the exact corrected numbers post-fix; 4 new regression tests added to `playwright/input_otp.spec.ts` (empty-field clamping, partially-filled clamping across all 6 slots, fully-filled exact positioning, disabled-state no-op) — full suite **11/11 passed** on Chromium (7 pre-existing + 4 new); independently re-confirmed by the orchestrating session with its own live click test against a running `dx serve` instance, matching the subagent's numbers exactly at every slot for empty/partially-filled/fully-filled values | Landed 2026-09-17 | S | User-reported, found while re-testing row 60's fix on the live site; delegated to a single Sonnet subagent (files: `primitives/src/input_otp.rs`, `playwright/input_otp.spec.ts` only, disjoint from row 62's concurrent lane), independently re-verified — cargo/lint, the full existing+new suite, and the orchestrating session's own from-scratch click-position measurements, not just the subagent's reported numbers — before landing |
| 64 | **Input OTP: clicking a slot and typing didn't correct that digit — LANDED 2026-09-17, user-reported.** Row 63 (same day) fixed the OTP input's click-to-caret mapping, but the caret it placed was a *collapsed* one — correct for positioning, wrong for the user's next step: typing over it. Reproduced live before writing any fix: on a full value `"123456"` (at `maxlength`), clicking slot 2 then typing `9` left the value completely unchanged — the keystroke was silently swallowed; on a partial value `"123"`, clicking slot 1 then typing `9` produced `"1923"` (inserted, shifting `"23"` right), not the expected `"193"` (that slot's digit replaced). **Cause:** a collapsed caret makes native typing *insert* at that position; native *replace* semantics only kick in when an actual selection range covers the character about to be typed over — and insert additionally hits a silent `maxlength` block outright once the value is already full, explaining why the full-value case did nothing at all. This is exactly why upstream `input-otp` never trusts a bare caret for this: **fixed** in the same `snap_caret_to_slot` eval (`primitives/src/input_otp.rs`, row 63's own function) by selecting the one character at the clicked slot — `setSelectionRange(clamped, clamped + 1, 'forward')` — whenever `clamped < value_len` (a real character sits there); when `clamped == value_len` (clicked past the last real character, the ordinary "continue typing" case), the collapsed-caret behavior stays, since there's nothing there to select. **Direction chosen deliberately:** `'forward'` (anchor at `clamped`, focus at `clamped + 1`), matching this component's own left-to-right reading order (`ArrowRight` increases the index, `Delete` already acts forward from the caret) and giving Shift+Arrow a sensible extend-direction if this control is ever driven that way — verified to have zero effect on the *unshifted* nav this module already tests, since unshifted `ArrowLeft`/`ArrowRight` on any selection collapse to that selection's edge regardless of stored direction (the pre-existing "Backspace/Delete/ArrowLeft/ArrowRight" test passes unmodified, confirming this). **Verified by execution, independently re-checked by the orchestrating session:** `cargo check --workspace`/`cargo fmt --check` clean; all three composition/class-prefix/css-literal lint scripts clean; a genuine `git stash`/unstash before/after comparison reproduced the exact broken values above pre-fix (`"123456"` unchanged, `"1923"`) and the exact corrected ones post-fix (`"129456"`, `"193"`); the empty-field and last-character-replace cases were also checked and behave correctly (clicking past an empty value's end still collapses to a plain caret, since nothing exists there to select; replacing the last filled character doesn't insert past `maxlength`); 4 new regression tests added to `playwright/input_otp.spec.ts` under a new "Click-then-type replace" block — full suite **15/15 passed** on Chromium (11 pre-existing unchanged + 4 new), independently re-run and re-confirmed by the orchestrating session with its own from-scratch click-then-type measurements at all three value states, matching the subagent's numbers exactly | Landed 2026-09-17 | S | User-reported, found while re-testing row 63's fix on the live site; delegated to a single Sonnet subagent (files: `primitives/src/input_otp.rs`, `playwright/input_otp.spec.ts` only, same ownership as row 63's lane, run sequentially after it rather than in parallel since it edits the exact same function), independently re-verified — cargo/lint, the full existing+new suite, and the orchestrating session's own from-scratch click-then-type measurements, not just the subagent's reported numbers — before landing |

One class: a doc comment used `[`ident`]` link syntax to casually mention an internal helper that is either private/`pub(crate)` or `#[cfg(feature = "web")]`-gated out of that exact build, and rustdoc treats every `[`ident`]` as a link it must resolve regardless of intent. 25 of the 26 downgraded from a link to plain `` `...` `` inline code (still reads as a named identifier, no false hyperlink claim); one (`resizable.rs`'s `[`ResizablePanel::default_size`]`) named a real, public, resolvable path that was simply wrong — repointed at `ResizablePanelProps::default_size`. | 68 | Cross-worktree cargo build-directory hazard | Found 2026-09-18, four independent occurrences | — | This session's `~/.cargo/config.toml` sets `build.target-dir` to one shared directory so parallel git-worktree lanes reuse compiled dependencies (`evaluation-draft.md`'s Environment notes) — intended as a speed optimization, but the sharing is not safe: cargo's own output-caching keyed the shared directory to another lane's build-script `OUT_DIR` and test binary, and **served them as if they were the querying lane's own**, not just slower-but-correct. **First found by Lane D (Resizable):** its own `cargo check -p preview`/`cargo test -p dioxus-primitives` runs in the shared directory silently picked up another lane's `preview` build-script `OUT_DIR` (containing `data_table`, a component Lane D never touched) and a foreign `dioxus-primitives` test binary — persistently, not a one-off flake. **Independently observed by three more lanes** (their own hand-backs each note re-verifying in an isolated `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` once this was known: Lane B/Drawer — "the shared one proved to leak state across concurrent worktrees"; Lane H/typeahead and Lane G/portal-toggle-calendar — "the shared build directory was found to serve cross-worktree-contaminated artifacts"; Lane C/Navigation Menu also switched to an isolated dir for the same reason) **and by integration round 1 itself**, the other direction: round 1's own gate run used the shared directory against its own instruction to use an isolated one, so its `cargo test --workspace`/`clippy` results are flagged in `round1-report.md` as "to be re-confirmed in round 2's isolated dir" rather than trusted outright. **Rule, stated once so it does not recur a fifth time:** one `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` per worktree, always — the shared-directory speed optimization is incompatible with concurrent lanes and should not be reintroduced without a mechanism that actually isolates build-script output and test binaries per worktree, not just per crate |
| 69 | Axe scan readiness race (`playwright/axe.ts`), baseline group B | Found + fixed 2026-09-18 (construction); live verification confirmed round 2 | S | `playwright/axe.ts`'s `expectNoAxeViolations()` called `AxeBuilder.analyze()` immediately after `page.goto()` resolved, with no readiness wait. For this CSR wasm app the `load` event can fire before Dioxus paints its first frame into the empty `<div id="main">` shell, so the scan occasionally raced the app and saw the bare `<html lang="en">` — axe then reported `landmark-one-main` + `page-has-heading-one` against `html` itself, a false violation: every failing test's own `error-context.md` snapshot, captured moments later, shows a fully-formed page with exactly one `<main>` and one `<h1>`. Evidence it is a race, not a docs-chrome defect (the task brief's own hypothesis — PRs #52-57's navbar/sidebar rewrite — was checked directly and does not hold): all 8 failures finished in 1.3-2.0s versus 1.8-8.5s for every *passing* axe scan in the same run — fast because there was almost nothing in the DOM yet to scan, not because the page was simpler. Members: `empty`, `alert`, `tabs` (tab 1), `collapsible` (collapsed), `toolbar`, `button_group`, `breadcrumb`, `avatar`. **Construction (Lane I, commit `3cb0b16`):** 72 call sites share `expectNoAxeViolations`, so the fix lives in the helper (`waitForAppReady`), not per spec file — wait for the page's one `<main>` landmark to attach, then for a level-one heading to attach (`state: "attached"` only, no `networkidle`/visibility wait), bounded at 15s so a page that genuinely never renders still fails fast. Two waits, not one, because the race produces two violations and every route this suite scans renders both together as part of the same first paint (verified by reading every route fn in `preview/src/main.rs`). **Not verified against a live browser by the lane** (a mid-task sandbox resource correction ruled out starting a dev server there) — checked only with `playwright test --list` (all 694 tests across 61 files still enumerate, no parse/type error introduced). Live re-run of the 8 Group-B specs plus the axe-adjacent failures this same race caused elsewhere (`native-dialog.spec.ts` Rule 6c/6d/6e, `top-layer.spec.ts` Rule 5 Tooltip/HoverCard — see row 70, same fixture, different mechanism) is confirmed: **round 2's targeted Playwright run against the integrated build found all 8 Group-B specs green**, plus the axe-adjacent failures this same race caused elsewhere. Red/green demonstrated directly, not just inferred: the pre-fix `axe.ts` re-run under 4 workers × 8 (Group B's 8 specs) failed 7/16 with the baseline signature; the current `axe.ts` under the same conditions is 16/16. `pw-baseline/BASELINE-REPORT.md` Group B is the full evidence trail |
| 70 | `top_layer` oracle fixture probes intercepted by the docs Sidebar, baseline group C | Found + fixed 2026-09-18 (construction); live verification confirmed round 2 | S | Every route now permanently mounts `DocsLayout`'s `Sidebar` (PRs #51-57 dogfooding the real Sidebar site-wide), whose real container (`.dx-sidebar-container`) is `position: fixed; z-index: var(--dx-z-local-sm)` (10), occupying a `left: 0`, 16rem-wide column at any viewport ≥768px — including chromium's default 1280×720. The `top_layer` fixture (`/component/?name=top_layer&`) places several of its own probe triggers with `position: fixed` at literal viewport edges and **no `z-index` of their own** (`z-index: auto`) — the near-bottom-edge-flip row and one inline-styled modal-anchor trigger both sit under the Sidebar's column. A `position: fixed` box with `z-index: auto` always loses hit-testing to a sibling stacking context with an explicit positive `z-index`, regardless of DOM order — so Playwright's real `.click()`/`.hover()` (not `.evaluate(el => el.click())`, which bypasses hit-testing and is what several *passing* sibling tests in the same describe blocks use) retried until the outer 5-minute test timeout, naming `.dx-sidebar-desktop`/`.dx-sidebar-group-label`/`.dx-sidebar-menu-button` as the interceptor in Playwright's own actionability log. Members: `native-dialog.spec.ts` Rule 6c/6d/6e (`#popover-modal-anchor-trigger`) and `top-layer.spec.ts` Rule 5 Tooltip/HoverCard flip tests. **Not a new mechanism** — the modal-anchor trigger's own comment already documents this fixture hitting the identical interception once before, against the navbar, fixed there by nudging `top`; this repo's "two occurrences is a class" rule applied: nudging position again would only fix this one collision and leave the mechanism free to recur against the next permanently-mounted chrome. **Construction (Lane I, commit `e17c996`):** raise the fixture's own affected wrappers to `z-index: var(--dx-z-top, 9999)` — the fixture's own existing "clear ordinary page chrome" convention (already applied to `.dx-top-layer-stack-sibling`) — instead of another position nudge; `.dx-top-layer-edge-right-col` (the opposite edge row) got the same clearance preventively, same mechanism, not observed failing at the current viewport but not guaranteed to hold at every one — one root cause subsuming both by construction rather than a per-trigger patch. Every probe's actual edge/offset geometry (what Rule 5's flip and Rule 6e's trigger-anchored placement measure) is untouched. **Not verified against a live browser by the lane** (same sandbox resource constraint as row 69) — checked only with `playwright test --list`. Live re-run of the 5 Group-C specs is confirmed: **round 2's targeted Playwright run against the integrated build found all 5 Group-C specs green** (`native-dialog.spec.ts` Rule 6c/6d/6e's `#popover-modal-anchor-trigger`, `top-layer.spec.ts` Rule 5 Tooltip/HoverCard flip tests) — fixed live, not just confirmed by `--list`. Same lane and same day as row 69 (Lane I); `pw-baseline/BASELINE-REPORT.md` Group C is the full evidence trail, including the confirming negative (sibling tests hitting a different trigger location on the identical page passed in 1.4-3.6s) |
| 71 | Rule 11 self-overlap regression, baseline group D | Found + fixed 2026-09-18; live re-run against the integration server confirmed round 2 | S | Found in the 2026-09-18 baseline (4 cases: Select/Combobox "no self-overlap once open", Navbar simulated-keyboard, DropdownMenu simulated-no-anchor-engine) — a discrepancy from `conformance-harness.md`'s own last-recorded-good state (Rule 11 landed 2026-09-02, file "passing" after Rule 15 landed 2026-09-04), not a previously-accepted flake. Root cause, bisected by git archaeology plus an instrumented reproduction (Lane J): commit `daebe40` (2026-09-14ish, PR #54, sidebar TOC) added a global `html { scroll-behavior: smooth }` to `preview/assets/main.css` for legitimate in-page TOC-link navigation. `top-layer.spec.ts`'s `pinNearTop` fixture helper calls `scrollIntoView`/`window.scrollTo` with no `behavior` option, so both silently started animating over several hundred ms instead of jumping instantly — breaking `pinNearTop`'s own documented contract of delivering the trigger at a settled position *before* `overlay.open()` runs. Confirmed by execution: the trigger's `getBoundingClientRect().top` kept changing for 400-600ms *after* `pinNearTop` had already resolved (Combobox: 643→541→93→78, settling ~400ms later); `use_anchor_position_fallback`'s rAF-throttled scroll listener is necessarily one step behind a continuous CSS-driven scroll animation under real scheduling load (this sandbox's concurrent WASM/Chromium contention especially) — a real geometry mismatch between two moments of the *same still-settling* trigger, not a placement-formula defect. One root cause subsumes every Rule 11 failure observed this session, baseline's four plus two more (Combobox(a)/Navbar(a)) caught independently by the lane's own full-suite reproduction — every sub-case calls `pinNearTop` before opening, and the numeric signature (content positioned for an *earlier* trigger reading) was identical across every case inspected. Bisection: `git log -S "scroll-behavior: smooth"` pins the regression to `daebe40` exactly (absent in its parent, present in it); an isolated, non-full-suite Rule 11 rerun did **not** reproduce even pre-fix — this is a timing race whose window opens wide enough only under real CPU contention, so the full `top-layer.spec.ts` suite under 2-worker load is the only reliable regression check for it, not an isolated Rule-11-only run. **Fix (Lane J, commit `55ec9b1`):** qualify both `pinNearTop` scroll calls with `behavior: "instant"` (CSSOM View's standard per-call opt-out of an ancestor's `scroll-behavior: smooth`), restoring the helper's pre-existing contract without touching the legitimate site-wide smooth-scroll feature. Deliberately kept out of `primitives/src/top_layer.rs`: the fallback's tracking is already exactly as good as a rAF-throttled listener can be against a continuous CSS animation it has no visibility into, and scroll-tracking fidelity during a genuine in-flight scroll is Rule 8's own separately-tracked, already-documented limitation (row 21), not Rule 11's. **Repo-wide class note:** any Playwright helper that calls `scrollTo`/`scrollIntoView` without an explicit `behavior` is implicitly coupled to the page's `scroll-behavior` CSS — true everywhere in this suite, not just `pinNearTop`. **Residual, not closed by this fix:** the vertical clamp's own trigger-blind edge-hugging branch remains a plausible independent latent gap — no evidence of it firing today, flagged as a follow-up, not chased further. Verified by execution, own dev server on :8082: full `top-layer.spec.ts` pre-fix 5 failed (2 Rule 11 + 2 Group C + 1 row 21) / post-fix, two consecutive runs, 3 then 2 failed, zero Rule 11 in either. **Live re-run against the shared integration server (not the lane's own isolated :8082) is confirmed: round 2's targeted Playwright run found group D (Rule 11) all green, including all 26 Rule 11 sub-assertions.** `pw-baseline/BASELINE-REPORT.md` Group D is the full evidence trail; distinct from row 21 (Rule 8's own anchor-freeze) and row 70/Group C (a hit-testing interception) — three different mechanisms on the same spec file, not one bug wearing three names |

| 73 | Drawer drag hang — self-subscribing `use_effect` wedged the main thread on drag | Found in integration round 2 (2026-09-18), fixed round 3 same day | S | Round 2's live integration run found 2 of 9 `drawer.spec.ts` tests hanging the 5-minute ceiling at the raw `page.mouse.down()` call, after the drawer opened and geometry was measured ("a long, slow drag past the dismiss threshold closes the drawer", "a short, slow drag snaps back without closing the drawer") — no panic, no console output; a follow-up `page.evaluate` hung too, proving the tab's whole main thread was wedged, not just the test. Root cause: `DrawerContent`'s drag-continuation `use_effect` read `raw_offset()` with the tracked call syntax (subscribing the running effect to that signal) and then, in the same execution, wrote a new value to that same signal via `.set()` — writing to a signal an effect just subscribed to reliably re-triggers it, and nothing on this path ever broke the cycle, so from the first `pointerdown` the effect re-invoked itself synchronously, forever. Fixed (commit `b7037cb`, cherry-picked from `896e603`): `.peek()` instead of the tracked read for both of the effect's self-referential reads of `raw_offset` (the release-decision read and the accumulation read), matching the read-through-`.peek()`-write-through-`.set()` discipline `lib.rs`'s `use_animated_open` already documents and uses for its own generation counter, for exactly this reason. Verified live, own server: before, `mouse.down()`/a follow-up `evaluate` both hung 5s+ with no recovery; after, both return immediately and the real `drawer.spec.ts` run is 7/7, including both drag tests (3.0s, 1.4s). **House-wide review rule this implies:** any `use_effect` that reads a signal with tracked syntax and then writes that same signal in the same run will re-trigger itself forever unless something in the same run breaks the cycle — worth a standing review checklist line, not just a fix on this one file. **Closed 2026-09-18/19, batch 2, row 79:** that standing checklist line is now `scripts/check-self-subscribing-effects.sh` (`f11b98b`), proven against this exact bug by failing on `cdb073b` (this row's own pre-fix commit) and passing once `b7037cb` (this row's own fix) is applied. **Family note (2026-09-19, batch 3):** a second, independent instance of the same defect *shape* (an unconditional reactive cycle wedging the main thread, found live in a Playwright run) recurred in `DateRangePicker` — see row 83. Different construction there (a value-equality guard on the write side, not a `.peek()` read), because that cycle spans multiple effects through a callback, not one effect's own tracked read+write |
| 74 | Navigation Menu first-render panic (hook called inside another hook's closure) + demo/site-chrome accessible-name collisions | Found in integration round 2 (2026-09-18), fixed round 3 same day | S | Round 2's live integration run found 7/7 `navigation_menu.spec.ts` tests failing (2 fast, 5 hanging the 5-minute ceiling) on `getByRole('button', { name: 'Getting started' })` never resolving, plus `disclosure-navigation.spec.ts`'s "Library" block failing the same way (its Reference-page block, R1-R5, passed). Root cause #1: `NavigationMenu`'s root component called `use_delayed_action()` (a hook wrapping `use_signal`, `menu_sub.rs`) three times *inside* the closure passed to `use_context_provider` — calling a hook from within another hook's initializer closure re-enters the scope's hook-list `RefCell` while it is already borrowed, panicking at runtime ("BorrowMutError: The hook list is already borrowed ... hook inside a hook", dioxus-core's `scope_context.rs`) on first render, a wasm `unreachable` trap before anything — not even `main`/`h1` — mounts. **This is a runtime-only invariant**: not `cargo check`, not clippy, not the doc tests catch it, which is why the original lane's own thorough verification never surfaced it. Because `ComponentGallery` (the home page, `/`) renders every demo to build its gallery, this one bad demo's panic took down the entire home page and every home-page test with it (native-dialog Rule 8's "Show Popover" lookup, `preview.spec.ts`, hydration-parity Rule 4b) — **the class: one bad demo takes down `/`**, worth a standing awareness for anything `ComponentGallery` renders. Fixed (commit `c3dda45`, cherry-picked from `963d630`): hoisted all three `use_delayed_action()` calls out of the closure into the component body; audited every other `use_context_provider`/`use_hook`/`use_memo`/`use_effect`/`use_callback` closure in the file, no other instance (every other closure only calls plain non-hook constructors, the same safe pattern `AccordionContext::new` already uses). Root cause #2, found by the same live reproduction: the demo's featured-card link text ("dioxus-components") and its "Docs" link both collide with accessible names the site's own persistent chrome already uses on every route (header navbar brand, footer), so unscoped `page.getByRole('link', { name: ... })` queries resolved 2-3 elements page-wide instead of 1. Fixed by construction, not per-locator: featured-card title renamed "Component Library"; both spec files scope every query to the demo's own `aria-label="Component navigation menu"` landmark via a `nav(page)`/`libraryNav(page)` helper — demo content must not reuse accessible names the persistent chrome already renders, or specs must scope to the demo landmark. Verified live (own server, direct Playwright-API script, coordinator's server being read-only): 22/22 — R1-R5 plus every smoke-spec assertion. **Closed 2026-09-18/19, batch 2, row 79:** root cause #1's exact class (a hook called inside another hook's closure) is now guarded by `scripts/check-hooks-in-closures.sh` (`f11b98b`), proven against this row's own before/after commits (fails on `bb65cd6`, passes on `c3dda45` and current `HEAD`). **Family note (2026-09-19, batch 3):** row 83's `DateRangePicker` hang is a related-but-distinct class from this row's own hook-in-closure panic — grouped together only because both were runtime-only defects a live Playwright run caught that no static check (`cargo check`, clippy, doctests) could |
| 75 | Per-variant `component-preview-frame` duplicate id + unscoped Table caption locator (two strict-mode violations, one class each) | Found in integration round 2 (2026-09-18), fixed round 3 same day | S | Round 2's live integration run found two independent Playwright strict-mode violations. (a) `popover.spec.ts`'s basic test (`page.locator("#component-preview-frame")`, no `.first()`) resolved 2 elements once the `non_modal` demo variant existed alongside `main`, because every "Normal" component page renders one `ComponentVariantHighlight` per variant (main plus each "Variants" entry) and each copy hard-coded the literal id `component-preview-frame` on its own "Demo" `TabContent` — pre-existing for every multi-variant component (calendar's 5 extra variants, sidebar's `floating`/`inset` block variants), just never queried by that bare id before. Fixed by construction, not per-instance (commit `80af124`, cherry-picked from `af6a8d6`), in both `ComponentVariantHighlight` (Normal-kind) and its Block-kind sibling `BlockComponentVariantHighlight` (same defect, same shape, confirmed by reading): `main` keeps `component-preview-frame`; every other variant gets `component-preview-frame-<name>`. Audited all 20 existing `playwright/` call sites (10 spec files) — calendar's 5 already used `.first()` wanting the main demo, a no-op change now correct by construction instead of DOM-order luck; popover's own basic test is the one this fixes. Verified live on the fixing lane's own server: the basic test, previously reproduced failing with the reported strict-mode violation, passes warmed up (1.2s). (b) `table.spec.ts`'s basic test used an unscoped `page.getByText("A list of your recent invoices.")`, which also appears as literal source text in the page's always-mounted "Code" tab (`ComponentVariantHighlight` renders both "Demo" and "Code" `TabContent`s as siblings regardless of which is active) and in the "Manual installation" `<details>` section — so the unscoped lookup resolved 2 elements. **The same class, not a one-off**: any unscoped `page.getByText()`/`getByRole(..., {name})` whose string also appears in the demo's own rendered source is ambiguous on every component page in this app; the lane audited `table.spec.ts`, `data_table.spec.ts` and `sortable-table.spec.ts` for the same shape and found every other lookup already scoped under an attribute-selected container or constrained by a role the syntax-highlighted code spans never carry. Fixed (commit `fdb88bb`, cherry-picked from `5023dea`): scoped to the real `<caption>` inside the `data-slot="table"`-selected table, asserting both visibility and exact text. | 76 | Dev-loop measurements and tooling (`dev-wait.sh`, `inspect.mjs`, `base-url.ts`) | Landed 2026-09-18 (Lane K, round 3) | S | Lane K (commit `89f8e07`, cherry-picked from `a9f651c`) measured the actual edit-to-feedback latency on this repo's `dx serve --web` (debug, 4-core/15GB, isolated `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`) rather than assuming it, and shipped tooling built on the measurements: an RSX text edit hot-reloads in ~0.5-0.8s, but — a newly found trap — **only to already-connected tabs**; a fresh page load or navigation (including a real Playwright test) keeps serving the old wasm regardless, confirmed by a held-open page vs. two independent fresh loads a minute apart. A component `style.css` edit under `src/` costs a full ~28s rebuild (only `preview/assets/` CSS hot-reloads, ~0.9s) — CSS under `src/` is not cheap here, contrary to a natural assumption. A Rust logic edit in a primitive is ~38s; a new module is ~85s end-to-end, subject to a second newly found trap (rapid multi-file saves can log a "successful" but incomplete rebuild before a trailing one actually lands the change). `dx serve --hot-patch` fails immediately on any Rust logic edit inside the `primitives` workspace crate ("Missing rustc args for replay: 'dioxus_primitives'") and stays broken for every subsequent edit on that server, even ones touching only `preview` — not usable as this workspace's default today. Delivered: `scripts/dev-wait.sh` (blocks on the dev server's log signal rather than the `/_dioxus/build_status` websocket, which exists and is documented but has a gap — no new message on a second hot-reload-only edit in a row — so log parsing stays primary), `scripts/inspect.mjs` (direct browser inspection — a11y/DOM outline, `--eval`, `--click`/`--press`, `--axe`, `--screenshot` — against a warm server in ~1-5s, no spec file, no full suite needed), and `playwright/base-url.ts` (a `PLAYWRIGHT_BASE_URL` env override consolidating three pre-existing ad hoc `BASE_URL`/`BASE` patterns into one, default behaviour unchanged; migrated as a proof of concept in avatar/toolbar/sidebar/drag_and_drop_list.spec.ts, verified live against a non-default port). Full write-up, the complete measurement table, and the trap catalogue: [`dev-docs/dev-loop.md`](./dev-loop.md) (not edited by this docs pass). **Partially superseded/closed 2026-09-18/19, batch 2:** the 4-spec proof-of-concept migration this row describes is now essentially complete, not just started — see row 81 (`e0bb23f`/`2cb1b06`/`3cc0881`, every remaining spec migrated) and row 80 (`238c8f4`, the CSS-hot-reload root cause this row's own measurement table first surfaced, corrected from "move the files" to "stop compiling them in") |
| 77 | User-reported UI round (2026-09-18/19, batch 2): Command palette focus, Data Table filter, Tag Group sizing, Sheet, Resizable, Navigation Menu reflow, Drawer ×3 | Landed 2026-09-18/19 (batch 2) — six independent user reports against components rows 66-76 had just shipped | M | **Six user reports, each root-caused and fixed independently; the batch-2 lane hand-backs and its integration report are this row's source, cited here as session scratchpad, not as a repo artifact.** **Command palette first-open focus** (`1e6ae0f`): `command.rs` keeps its existing `autofocus` on the input and adds an `onmounted` → `spawn(set_focus(true))` backstop, mirroring the pattern `toggle`/`slider`/`dropdown_menu` already use, plus a forwarding `onmounted` prop. Root cause unconfirmed — the defect never reproduced on the CSR `dx serve` dev server across 40+ trials; the original report was against the deployed SSG+hydration build, so the backstop covers the class regardless. Follow-up: execute the SSG lane (row 22) to confirm against the build shape that actually showed it — batch 3. **Data Table filter question:** no defect — the existing predicate already matches shadcn's TanStack `includesString` (trimmed, case-insensitive substring). `0c0f627` gives the existing behaviour a name (`state.rs`'s `includes_string`, exported from `component.rs`) and 8 unit tests, changing nothing about how it matches; the dashboard email client's `filters.rs` has the identical shape, left untouched as a candidate for a future shared helper. **Closed 2026-09-19 (batch 3 integration, `b22a222`):** `filters.rs`'s `message_matches_search` now routes its five `.to_lowercase().contains(&query)` sites through `crate::components::data_table::includes_string` instead of its own manual trim+lowercase local, dropping the duplication this row's own text flagged — and fixing a latent bug along the way, found by the leftovers lane while proposing the refactor: `tag.label().contains(&query)` never lowercased the label side, so a mixed-case tag search silently under-matched. **Tag Group sizing** (`090d9f1`): confirmed NOT a token-migration regression (row 31b) — an audit of all 41 stylesheets row 31b touched found 363 exact-value matches and 130 equal-value unit rewrites, 0 mismatches, and upstream `bf007c1` renders the identical 20px tag today; the real, pre-existing gap is `text-sm` sizing plus `padding-block: var(--dx-space-1)` and `min-height: var(--dx-space-7)` under `border-box`, now rendering a 28px-tall tag (was 20px). **Sheet** (`12a8f86`): a shadcn/ui v4 translation pass — overlay tint moved onto the `<dialog>`'s own `::backdrop` on the web arm (the UA's `dialog::backdrop` opacity was stacking with the root tint, measuring ~55% instead of shadcn's 50%), `--dx-shadow-lg`, `sm:max-w-sm` gated to `@media (width >= 40rem)`, a 500/300ms slide, close-button `size-4`/`rounded-xs` — plus the Drawer-class centering fix, see row 78. **Resizable** (`47c6291`): the handle/grip were reading the wrong token pair (`--secondary-color-3/4`, a foreground-ish gray) instead of the border pair every other divider in this app uses (`--primary-color-6/7`); handle width corrected 4px → 1px (with a 4px `after:` hit area), grip 16×24px → 12×16px, demo container gained `max-w-md md:min-w-[450px]` (was unconstrained at ~269px against shadcn's 450px reference). Deliberate deviation, not a gap: shadcn's `h-[200px]` fixed content height is NOT translated, because this primitive's panels are pure-CSS flex-basis percentages over `overflow: hidden`, not react-resizable-panels' JS-measured layout — a fixed height clipped the nested 25% panel, caught by screenshot before it shipped. **Navigation Menu "width reflow"** (`08a14d8`): not a width transition — rAF sampling showed a constant width throughout; the panel-swap animation was dead, so switching between differently sized panels popped instantly, read by the reporter as a layout reflow. Two independent causes: (1) the `transition` rule, at the normal cascade tier, can never outrank the INLINE `transform: none` that `use_anchor_position_fallback` sets on every render (Navigation Menu sets no `data-side`/`data-align`, so the JS positioning fallback always engages, even on engines with native CSS Anchor Positioning); (2) the web arm's `use_popover_sync` calls `hidePopover()` synchronously on close, removing the content before any exit animation gets a frame. Fixed with `@keyframes`/`animation` (outranks an inline style) plus `use_popover_shown_while_mounted` (the same hook Tooltip/HoverCard/Popover/`SelectList`/`ComboboxList` already use for this exact reason, row 19) and `width: max-content`, gated by `prefers-reduced-motion`. The same commit adds the keyboard surface row 72 had deliberately left out (Home/End, panel Up/Down stopping at the ends, ArrowDown opening a closed trigger and focusing its first item) and fixes three latent bugs the keyboard work surfaced: content children mounting in reverse source order (now an explicit caller-supplied `content_index` prop), native Tab focus never syncing into the roving-focus collection (`collection.set_focus`, matching toolbar/tabs), and a stale-value no-op in the Escape-refocus handler. **Follow-up, outside this row's ownership:** `navbar.rs` pairs the identical `use_animated_open` + `use_popover_sync` combination, so it likely carries the same dead exit-animation defect (cause 2 above) — not investigated or fixed here. **Drawer, ×3 fixes, one shared construction** (`7310b89` + `2bf6489`): (1) the native `dialog`/`dialog:modal` UA stylesheet centers the panel as a floating card instead of anchoring it to its edge — the SAME class as Sheet's centering bug above, see row 78 for the shared construction and the sweep of the rest of `dialog.rs`'s consumers; the overdrag gap the fix leaves is covered by a solid `filter: drop-shadow` extension 40px past the edge (a `box-shadow`/`::after` would be clipped by the panel's own `overflow`). (2) `user-select: none` on the drag handle and on content while dragging, and the handle's `touch-action` corrected from `none` to a per-side `pan-*` value. (3) `DrawerClose` had no styling at all, so dark mode rendered the UA `ButtonFace` background at a measured 1.28:1 contrast — now styled like `.dx-button[data-style="outline"]`. `2bf6489` additionally makes the drag-continuation effect read `active_pointer_id` with `.peek()` (row 79) while keeping `dragging` under a tracked read as the effect's own wake switch. All six were verified live against the shared `:8080` server during batch-2 integration (per-lane table in the integration report, cited here only as session scratchpad, not re-run by this docs pass) |
| 78 | The native `<dialog>` un-freed-opposite-edge centering class | Landed 2026-09-18/19 (batch 2, Drawer + Sheet); swept clean elsewhere (batch 3) | S | **One construction, two instances this round, one clean sweep.** Both `Drawer` (`7310b89`) and `Sheet` (`12a8f86`) hit the identical bug shape independently: a per-side CSS rule overrides exactly ONE inset edge (e.g. `inset-inline-end: 0` on a right-anchored panel) while the UA's `dialog:modal` stylesheet's own `margin: auto` is left free to center the *opposite*, un-overridden edge — so the browser renders a floating, centered card instead of an edge-anchored panel. Measured on Sheet before the fix: a `[data-side="right"]` sheet's left edge at `x=440.5` against a 1280px-wide viewport (should sit flush right), and a `[data-side="bottom"]` sheet computing an 800px-tall box (should sit flush to the viewport's own height). Fixed identically in both: reset `margin: 0; inset: 0; width: auto; height: auto` as the shared base, then let each `data-side` variant re-open exactly the one edge it needs, so no UA default is ever left to fill a gap the component didn't ask for. **Swept for the same shape elsewhere, measured clean** — a concurrent batch-3 lane checked every other web-arm consumer of `primitives/src/dialog.rs` for the identical un-freed-opposite-edge shape (reported here as session scratchpad, not independently re-run by this docs pass): `Dialog` and `AlertDialog` both set `margin: 0` explicitly (killing the UA's auto-margin centering outright) and position via `top/left: <pct>; transform: translate(-50%, -50%)`, a self-contained technique with no dependency on the UA's un-set edges; `Command`'s command-palette dialog uses the same technique as `Dialog`; modal `Popover` uses `top_layer.rs`'s `ensure_anchor_positioning_styles`, which resets all four `inset` edges to `auto` at once, not one at a time. All four measured correctly centered/anchored at `:8080`, cross-checked against `getBoundingClientRect()` (every box's measured center matched its expected center exactly). One harmless, consistent artifact on all four: `getComputedStyle(...).right`/`.bottom` still report the dead UA-inherited value even though `getBoundingClientRect()` proves it unused in layout — an over-constrained `left`+`width`+`margin` box reporting a stale computed value, not a rendering bug, and not this class. **Subsumes:** Drawer, Sheet (both fixed by the shared construction above). **Does not subsume, because the shape never applied:** Dialog, AlertDialog, Command, modal Popover — swept and confirmed clean, a different centering technique entirely with no gap for this class to occur in |
| 79 | Reactivity guards: `check-hooks-in-closures.sh` + `check-self-subscribing-effects.sh` | Landed 2026-09-18/19 (batch 2) | M | **Two new source-level guard scripts, joining the four already listed in `CLAUDE.md`** (`f11b98b`): `scripts/check-hooks-in-closures.sh` (no hook may be called from inside another hook's own initializer closure — the runtime-only panic class row 74 found only by execution, since neither `cargo check` nor clippy nor the doc tests see it) and `scripts/check-self-subscribing-effects.sh` (no `use_effect` may take a tracked read of a signal it also writes in the same run — the class row 73 found only by execution, hanging the main thread). The allowlisting was done anyway, per an explicit integration instruction that itself paraphrased that same reasoning before still directing the allowlist. Left unreconciled here on purpose: a human should decide whether `drawer.rs`'s comment gets a follow-up acknowledging the allowlist now exists, or whether the allowlist should instead be reverted in favor of a permanently-red, explained exception — a pass/fail script cannot represent that third option today. | 80 | CSS hot reload root cause: a compile-time embed, not the folder location | Landed 2026-09-18/19 (batch 2); supersedes row 72's "move stylesheets" bullet and row 76's number | M | **`238c8f4`.** Root cause: `dioxus_code::code!()` expands to `include_str!`, so a themed component's CSS text is compiled directly into the `preview` binary and lands in rustc's own dep-info; `dx`'s file watcher (`runner.rs::handle_file_change`) checks a changed non-`.rs` file against that dep-info list and, on a match, forces `needs_full_rebuild`, overriding that same file's own `asset!()` hot-reload path — the CSS is both compiled in and asset-served, and the compiled-in copy wins the watcher's decision every time. `build.rs`'s blanket `rerun-if-changed=src/components` was a compounding second cause, proven separately: narrowing it alone still produced a full rebuild, so it was not the sole mechanism. The same defect exists for `THEME_CSS` (`preview/assets/dx-components-theme.css`), which disproves row 76's original "CSS under `src/` is not cheap, `preview/assets/` CSS hot-reloads" theory — the mechanism is the compile-time embed, not which directory the file lives in. **Construction:** the `code!()` embed stays for release/SSG builds (`#[cfg(not(debug_assertions))]`, byte-identical output — not verified live in a browser, compile+lint only), so nothing changes for the shipped artifact; debug builds instead carry the `asset!()` URL and fetch-plus-syntax-highlight the CSS at runtime (`preview/src/main.rs`'s new `LazyCssCodeBlock`), so the watcher's dep-info list no longer contains the CSS text at all and the file's own `asset!()` hot-reload path is free to win. Measured: a `kbd/style.css` edit went from a 45.9s full rebuild to a 0.8s hot-reload, confirmed against a fresh, never-before-opened page load (not just an already-open tab, `dev-loop.md`'s own Trap 4). Checks: `fmt`, `check`/`clippy` in both debug and release, `cargo test --workspace` 23/23 (this component), all guards, stylelint, `preview.spec.ts` +1 ("style tab") 3/3. **Supersedes, in place:** row 72's "move component stylesheets so CSS edits hot-reload" bullet (the diagnosis there was directionally right about the symptom but named the wrong variable — moving files would not have fixed it, since `THEME_CSS` already lived in `preview/assets/` and had the identical defect) and row 76's "~28s" rebuild number (now historical: the same edit class is ~0.8s post-fix). `dev-loop.md` already carries this lane's own full write-up in its "CSS root cause, corrected" section — see there rather than duplicating it here |
| 81 | Fixtures-72 follow-ups, base-URL migration completion, and three full-suite-only test-timing fixes | Landed 2026-09-18/19 (batch 2) | M | **Row 72's two cheapest bullets, closed** (`8f3b10e`): `NavigationMenu` gets the identical 7-location oracle footprint `Navbar` already has in `top-layer.spec.ts` (Rule 1, axe, Rule 5, Rule 11 a/b + `CONFORMING_CASES` marked `supported: false`, Rule 13, Rule 14) via the same themed-wrapper fixture instance, and a raw-primitive `Select` fixture case with `scroll_lock: false` was added to `scroll-lock.spec.ts`, exercising row 9's opt-out prop for the first time; building the two cases found and fixed two methodology bugs along the way (the scroll-lock case's own `assertScrollIsUnlocked` helper, and an `evaluate(el => el.click())` call that bypassed real hit-testing). **Base-URL migration, complete** (`e0bb23f` + `2cb1b06` + `3cc0881`): every spec in the suite now honours `PLAYWRIGHT_BASE_URL` (`playwright/base-url.ts`, row 76's own proof-of-concept). `e0bb23f` finished the 10 spec files the original migration (row 76) had deliberately excluded because their owning lanes hadn't landed yet, minus the 2 navigation_menu-owned specs, which had to wait for the navmenu lane; `2cb1b06` migrated 8 of those, and `3cc0881` finished the last 2 (`navigation_menu.spec.ts`, `oracle/tier1-apg/disclosure-navigation.spec.ts`) once navmenu landed. A repo-wide grep for the old hardcoded literal now turns up only `base-url.ts`'s own default/doc comment, one pre-existing prose mention in a header comment, and `ssg.local.config.ts`'s own unrelated doc comment — every executable occurrence is migrated. Recipe, one line (full account in `dev-loop.md`): set `PLAYWRIGHT_BASE_URL` to point Playwright at any already-running server instead of the default `:8080`. **Three test-timing fixes found only by the contended full suite, not by any lane's own lighter-loaded run.** `3416551`: the shared `escapesClip` helper (`top-layer.spec.ts`, 11 call sites — native, Tooltip, HoverCard, Popover, DropdownMenu, ContextMenu, Menubar, Select, Combobox, Navbar, NavigationMenu) probed `document.elementFromPoint` immediately after `toBeVisible()`, with no wait for the probed element's own entrance animation — latent since the helper was written, because every one of those 11 subjects' entrance animations were themselves dead/instant until now; `NavigationMenu`'s own fix (row 77, `08a14d8`) was the first to make one genuinely run long enough to lose the race. Fixed in the helper itself, protecting all 11 callers: await `content.getAnimations({subtree: true})` before measuring. `f40c2b3`: Sheet's own new close-button test (row 77) measured the button's opacity and position immediately after `data-state` flipped, before the 500ms slide-in settled — the same fix, applied to the one caller that needed it directly. `4f05f75`: the drawer short-drag test's single `{ steps: 2 }` mouse move produced two internal samples with no timing guarantee, and when they landed under 1ms apart, `drawer.rs`'s own `elapsed_ms.max(1.0)` division guard (not a tuning knob) turned a near-instant step into an artificially explosive velocity, above the dismiss threshold — the file's own long-drag test already avoided this with an explicit per-step pause; the short-drag test now does too. `f7987aa`: `oracle/tier1-apg/sortable-table.spec.ts`'s DataTable R1 case read `sortableHeaders.all()` (a one-shot, non-retrying snapshot) before the CSR app's wasm bundle had rendered the table at all, unprotected by the auto-retrying `toBeVisible()`-style call every sibling test in the file happens to make first — fixed with one `expect(...).toBeAttached()` before the existing snapshot. **The lesson, worth stating plainly for the next round:** a lane's own green run against a lighter-loaded, single-purpose server is necessary but not sufficient evidence for a new Playwright test with any gesture or animation timing in it — full-suite contention is where these three surfaced, and none were flakes in the sense of being unfixable; each had a real, missing wait. The `getAnimations({subtree: true})` "wait for the entrance animation before measuring" idiom now exists in three independent places (`toast.spec.ts`, pre-existing; `escapesClip`; Sheet's close-button test) — worth extracting to a shared helper once a fourth caller needs it; not done here, since it would mean touching `toast.spec.ts`, an unrelated file with no defect of its own, for a pure refactor |
| 82 | Batch-2 integration outcome: full suite 775/12/11, net 0 unaddressed regressions | Landed 2026-09-19 (batch-2 integration, HEAD `f7987aa`) | — | **19 commits (12 lane picks + 7 integration-authored) landed on `claude/roadmap-evaluation-6eiahc`, base `cbae695`.** Gates green in an isolated `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`: the 6 `scripts/check-*.sh` guards (4 pre-existing + the 2 new ones from row 79), `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo check --workspace --all-targets` and `--features web`, `cargo test --workspace` 326/0/2, `cargo clippy --workspace --tests --examples -- -D warnings`, `cd preview && npx stylelint "src/**/*.css"`, and all three `cargo doc` invocations. nothing in batch 2 touched them). **8 known, unchanged:** date-picker anchoring — **both titles named explicitly**, per row 21's own updated exclusion list — "anchors the popup next to its trigger, not viewport-centered or offset" and "offset to trigger is unchanged after scrolling (CSS anchor tracks scroll natively)", the second reclassified into row 21's class this round rather than treated as new; all cargo-gate evidence was already captured by that point). **New dx-serve trap, added to `dx-serve-hot-reload.md` as trap 6** (cross-referenced from `dev-loop.md`): near-simultaneous saves to two different files can make the watcher drop the rebuild entirely — no `Hotreloading:`, no `Build completed`, the process idles at ~2% CPU — rather than merely mis-time it (distinct from `dev-loop.md`'s existing traps 4 and 5). A trivial re-save of either file recovers it; observed once, on the shared dev server during integration — not restarted, per this task's own non-negotiable rule, but nudged with a trivial add/remove blank-line edit, which fired a rebuild ~20s later that picked up every pending change |
| 83 | DateRangePicker page hang — `DateElement`'s sync-down/sync-up effects form an unconditional two-way reactive cycle | Found and fixed 2026-09-19 (batch 3, date-picker + range-hang lanes) | S | **Same family as rows 73/74** (a reactive-cycle/hook-discipline defect that froze the whole tab, found live during a Playwright run, not by any static check). Two page hangs, confirmed to be ONE mechanism: (1) typing a complete year+month+day into a `DateRangePicker`'s own segment inputs (no popover involved) hangs the JS thread forever, moments after the demo logs the correct value; (2) opening a range popover, clicking a start day then an end day (completing the range) hangs identically. Root cause: `DateElement`'s sync-down effect (reads `props.selected_date`, writes the segment's year/month/day) and its sync-up effect (reads the three segments, calls `on_date_change`, which writes `start_date`/`end_date` back) form an unconditional two-way cycle, and `Signal::set` (dioxus-signals 0.7.9, `*self.write() = value`) marks dirty and reschedules subscribers **unconditionally** — so once the cycle completes, each hop re-triggers the next, forever. Evidence: the renderer measured at 97-125% CPU with RSS growing +4MB/s unbounded; CDP `Profiler`/`Debugger` commands never returned (the render thread never yields — a synchronous wasm loop, not a slow one). The single `DatePicker`/`RangeCalendar` alone never hung, because `DatePickerContext::set_date`/`DateRangePickerContext::set_range` already guarded against an equal write. Construction (`b66d00e`): one guarded choke point, `DateRangeInputContext::set_start_date`/`set_end_date` (`if *self.start_date.peek() != date { ... }`), every producer of a start/end write now routed through it, mirroring the equality guard the context-level setters already had one layer up — no change needed to `calendar.rs`/`popover.rs`/`top_layer.rs`. Verified (`c899951`): both of `date-picker.spec.ts`'s own `test.fail()` hang guards now pass; the full targeted 22-file batch finished in 3.9 minutes (a hang would have burned the whole per-test timeout); re-confirmed in batch-3 integration's own runs, every time. **Standing rule, proposed by this finding, not yet automated:** any write that closes a reactive cycle must be conditioned on the value actually changing, because `Signal::set` never is by itself. `scripts/check-self-subscribing-effects.sh` (row 79) does **not** catch this class — verified directly, it reports clean on the pre-fix file, because this is a multi-effect cycle closed through a `Callback`/method call, not one effect's own tracked-read-then-write the existing heuristic looks for. A wider check (whole-file read/write set plus `Callback` resolution plus guarded-vs-unconditional detection) is specified but **not built**: a narrower heuristic risks false-positiving on every legitimate parent-notification effect in the codebase — left as a review-only rule until a real construction is designed |
| 84 | Date picker — a11y/UX gaps found but not fixed this round | **Landed 2026-09-20 (round 4, `c03f021`)** — all six findings closed in one pass | S–M | Six findings from the same investigation that landed row 20's closure, recorded rather than fixed (out of that round's own scope): (1) `Calendar`'s unavailable days carry no `aria-disabled` (`calendar.rs`); (2) the month segment has no `aria-valuetext`, so a screen reader announces a bare number instead of a month name; (3) `aria-valuenow` on an untouched segment shows today's date before the person has chosen anything, rather than being absent/indeterminate; (4) Up/Down arrow wraps a segment's value (including the year segment, where wrapping is almost never wanted) instead of clamping; (5) the demo's own i18n language switcher is dead code — `main.rs`'s `i18n().set_language(id)` call is commented out; (6) the first Up/Down keypress on a segment that has never been touched commits that segment's default value with no visible change on screen, which reads as a missed keypress. None of these block row 20's own closure; filed together since one future pass on this primitive's remaining a11y surface can address all six at once. **Closed 2026-09-20 (round 4, `c03f021`):** findings 2, 3 and 6 turned out to be one construction, not three independent fixes — `aria-valuenow`/`aria-valuetext` now mirror `props.value`'s own `Option` faithfully (present once the segment actually holds a value, omitted while it doesn't) instead of substituting `props.default` the way the old `unwrap_or(props.default)` did; that alone fixes finding 3 (no more leaked default) and finding 6 as its direct corollary — an untouched segment's `aria-valuenow` was already reporting the default before any keypress, so the first Up/Down press committing that same default looked like a dropped keystroke, and once "no value" stops being reported as "= default" that same first press becomes a genuine attribute-absent-to-present transition. Finding 2's locale-aware month name rides the same "has a value" gate, via a new `on_format_month: Callback<Month, String>` threaded down to `DatePickerMonthSegment` (and the DateRangePicker's mirror path), defaulting to `Month::to_string()` — the same default `DatePickerCalendarProps` already uses for the grid. Finding 4 became an explicit per-segment wrap/clamp policy (`SegmentValueBounds.wrap`) rather than a blanket fix, calibrated against Chromium's own native `<input type="date">` and the APG spinbutton pattern: month/day stay cyclical (`roll_value`), the year segment now clamps at its bound (`clamp_value`) instead of jumping to the opposite end, since a year is not cyclical and Chromium's own year sub-field does not wrap either. Finding 1 is a direct fix, `aria-disabled` added to `calendar_day_attributes`, mirroring `data-disabled` exactly (same source value, same unconditional presence). Finding 5's i18n switcher is now wired rather than deleted — `main.rs`'s `i18n().set_language(id)` call, previously commented out, is live (a contained two-line fix: import `i18n`, make the call), per this task's own stated preference for a working control over a deleted one. Evidence: every finding got a Playwright test confirmed RED on the unmodified tree (via a git-stash round-trip, so RED is the real base commit, not reasoning) and GREEN after, plus new Rust SSR/unit tests in both `calendar.rs` and `date_picker.rs` (the wrap-vs-clamp unit test additionally verified red by temporarily reverting just that branch); one pre-existing test (the non-digit-keystroke guard) needed updating for the now-legitimate `aria-valuenow === null` case, no other collateral hits found. Left deliberately alone, unrelated: the two pre-existing row-21 popover-anchoring failures (Chromium's own anchor-freeze) |
| 85 | DropdownMenu/ContextMenu — a raw DOM `.focus()` on a sub-trigger closes the whole menu | **Landed 2026-09-20 (round 4, `3ba793b`)** — fixed by construction across DropdownMenu, Menubar, and both Sub levels | S to verify | Not an RTL defect — reproduces identically on the untouched `main`/LTR variant of both `DropdownMenu` and `ContextMenu`, found only because this round's own RTL oracle exercises submenus more thoroughly than any prior spec. Calling `.focus()` directly on a submenu's own sub-trigger element (as a test driving focus programmatically would, and as an assistive-technology focus-restoration path plausibly could) closes the entire menu and reverts focus to the ROOT trigger, rather than simply focusing the sub-trigger in place. Not investigated further or fixed this round (out of rtl-rust's own remit); worth a dedicated look at `menu_sub.rs`'s focus-out/blur handling the next time either menu family is touched. **Closed 2026-09-20 (round 4, `3ba793b`), root-caused as a class rather than the single instance this row named:** every affected site shared one shape — a synchronous `onblur` deciding "did focus leave" by reading a Rust-tracked roving-focus signal (`ctx.focus.any_focused()` / `menu_ctx.focus.any_focused()` / `sub.focus.any_focused()`) that only this crate's own click/keyboard paths ever write (state-first, DOM-focus-second), and that no item in this "menu button" pattern family reconciles via its own `onfocus` — so a focus move arriving any other way (a test's raw `.focus()` call, or an assistive-technology focus-restoration path) read stale "nothing focused" state and closed the whole menu. `DropdownMenuTrigger`'s root and, independently, `MenubarTrigger`/`MenubarItem` and the shared submenu level (`DropdownMenuSubTrigger`/`ContextMenuSubTrigger`, `menu_sub.rs`'s `SubMenuState`) all carried the identical shape — Menubar's was the most directly reproducible of all (arrow-down onto an item then `.focus()` on its own trigger closed the content unconditionally, no mouse-opened precondition needed). **`ContextMenu`'s own root — this row's original, named instance — could not be reproduced red in this pass**: it already dismissed via `use_outside_dismiss` (DOM-containment-based, immune to the same-tick blur-before-focus ordering hazard by construction), so this row's original claim of a ContextMenu-root defect does not hold up under direct testing, stated plainly rather than smoothed over. Fixed by construction anyway, for uniformity: `DropdownMenu`'s root and `Menubar`'s own per-menu wrapper now get the identical `use_outside_dismiss` construction `ContextMenu`'s root already had (a new `root_id` on each), plus a new two-id variant (`menu_sub::use_sub_outside_dismiss`, since a `Sub`'s trigger and content are two siblings, never one wrapped element) for the shared submenu level in both `DropdownMenu.Sub` and `ContextMenu.Sub`; every displaced `onblur` branch was removed rather than left as a second, still-fragile path to the same conclusion. The new `ContextMenu`/`ContextMenu.Sub` tests are documented in-file as construction-parity, not a reproduced defect. **`navigation_menu.rs` shares the identical underlying constraint** (its own module doc already cites the same `dioxus-html` 0.7.9 missing-`related_target` gap this fix cites) but already answers it with a different, correct construction of its own — a debounced close plus every element's own `onfocus` cancelling it, robust to an external `.focus()` call by construction — checked during this investigation and deliberately left untouched. Evidence: RED confirmed for `DropdownMenu` (plain item and sub-trigger) and `Menubar`, exact before/after `data-state`/DOM-count values in the commit body; full existing suites re-verified green (`dropdown-menu.spec.ts` 2/2+axe, `context-menu.spec.ts` 13/13, `menubar.spec.ts` 3/3+axe, `oracle/tier1-apg/menu-submenu.spec.ts` 16/16, `keyboard-matrix.spec.ts`+`menu-roles.spec.ts`+`focus-restore-reference.spec.ts` 87/88 with one pre-existing intentional skip); `cargo test --workspace` 433/0. **This row's own landing then introduced a regression, found and fixed the same round:** `3ba793b` gave `DropdownMenu`'s root `div` and `MenubarMenu`'s own wrapping `div` a literal `id: root_id.cloned()` (plus, on `MenubarMenu`, a literal `role:`) beside the unmerged `..props.attributes` spread already on that element, with a comment claiming a caller-supplied `id` "still wins (spread order)" — false: SSR emits both, a WHATWG duplicate-attribute parse error (`dev-docs/conformance-harness.md` hydration-parity Rule 4, extended). It shipped into the prerendered site as `docs/component/top_layer/index.html`'s `<div id="dxc-1091" ... id="clip-dropdown-menu-root" ...>`, caught by `playwright/oracle/hydration-parity.spec.ts`'s "Rule 4 (extended)" when the main loop ran it against a freshly regenerated site after round-4 integration. Fixed by `f2be1d7` (`fix(menu): dedupe the id/role duplicate 3ba793b introduced on DropdownMenu/Menubar roots`): both elements' full literal-attribute sets now route through `merge_attributes`, the same construction `context_menu.rs`'s own root already uses (`b35d671`). Two new Rust SSR tests (one per component) assert a caller's `id` renders exactly once — both proven RED on the pre-fix tree (2 `id="` occurrences, e.g. `<div id="dxc-0" ... id="my-menu-root">`) and GREEN after (1) — and the fix was re-verified end-to-end against the documented SSG recipe (`dx build --ssg --features fullstack --platform web`, no `--base-path`, served at `/` on :8090, `SSG_SITE_DIR` set): `oracle/hydration-parity.spec.ts` **11/11 green**, the fixture now carrying a single `id="clip-dropdown-menu-root"`. **Corrects this row's own evidence above:** the "full existing suites re-verified green" list this row cites (`dropdown-menu.spec.ts`, `menubar.spec.ts`, etc.) ran CSR-only against a live `dx serve` dev server and could never have caught this regardless of how thoroughly it was run — the browser DOM coalesces sequential duplicate `setAttribute` calls into one, so the second attribute is only ever visible in SSR/SSG's raw text output; the hydration-parity SSG lane is the only thing in the whole harness able to catch this class at all |
