Also note the filename trap that surfaced it: `npx playwright test carousel.spec.ts` substring-matches BOTH this file and `playwright/carousel.spec.ts`; pass the absolute path to scope a run to one. **FIXED 2026-09-25 (round 8):** took the second option above — scoped the reference axe scan to `#ex1` (the carousel widget itself) via `expectNoAxeViolations`'s own `include` option, rather than editing the vendored reference page at all. Root cause was timing-dependent, not structural: the reference's own script populates `<pre><code id="sc1">` relative to when the scan runs, which is why the violation only showed up in a combined run and not in `carousel.spec.ts` alone — scoping the scan to the widget removes the class regardless of that timing. |

| 102 | Port the carousel edge rubber-band into `primitives/` as mode B3 (edge transform) — the component still behaves as mode A (clamped) | Owner reported twice that you cannot drag past slide 1 or the last slide and get a rubber-band; five mechanisms benched side by side 2026-09-22/23. **LANDED 2026-09-25 (round 8, `188e50b`, `c59dc8b`, `51a96a6`)** | M — the mechanism is settled and the invariants are written; the work is the port, not the design | **Design closed, implementation open.** Full write-up in [`dev-docs/research/carousel-overscroll-2026-09-23.md`](./research/carousel-overscroll-2026-09-23.md), with the bench checked in beside it. Root cause of every glitch across ~20 bench revisions is ONE thing: during a wheel/trackpad gesture the scroll position has **three would-be writers** — the compositor's momentum animation (where `preventDefault` is only advisory), the snap engine on its own schedule, and our code — and two of the three are neither observable nor sequenceable from JS. The negative evidence is decisive: the *mouse-drag* path stayed clean for 20+ revisions untouched, because a mouse drag has no native scrolling and our code is then the only writer. **THE RULE:** where the browser is already the scroller, never write the scroll position and never disable snapping — express the overdrag as a transform only; where there is no native scrolling, owning it is safe. Nuance for the port: the contest exists only while momentum is live, so a spring-back ease may own the offset once a burst is spent — detecting that end accurately is the remaining difficulty. **B3 is the choice and the reason is arithmetic, not taste:** B2 (damped margin) cannot resist, because damping means slowing the scroller, which means owning its position, which is exactly the fight the rule removes — with the fight gone its room fills 1:1 with raw travel, measured as a 25px/event gesture consuming the whole 260px room while 145 and 121px/event gestures at the other edge produced 3px and 0px. B3 has no wall: depth is bounded by the platform curve's asymptote (`L = track width / 0.55` = 582px on a 320px track) instead of by a `padding` value. Mode C felt closest to native and is subsumed by B3 — same mechanism on the track rather than the root, which is what dissolves C's z-index problem. Eight binding port invariants are listed in the research doc §6, each one the generalisation of a named bench regression (never `scrollLeft`; edge test synchronous from layout gaps; no smooth `scrollIntoView` for spring-back; never restart a spring-back in flight; no spacer children; suspend `scroll-snap-type` once per gesture; never change measurement and timing in the same revision; no pixel threshold below the device quantum). **LANDED 2026-09-25 (round 8):** ported as mode B3, per the rule above. The pointer-drag path keeps owning `scrollBy` (there is no native scrolling to contest there: it requests the full delta and treats any refusal as overdrag). A new, separate wheel/trackpad bridge, `use_carousel_wheel_bounce`, never calls `scrollBy`/`scrollLeft`/`scrollTop`/`preventDefault`/touches `scroll-snap-type` — edge detection is synchronous from `getBoundingClientRect` gaps, and overdrag is expressed only as a transform on `CarouselContent` itself (not the root — this is what dissolves mode C's z-index problem, per the research doc). Both paths write `style.transform` via `eval` outside the vdom, so hydration parity is unaffected; `prefers-reduced-motion` disables the bounce entirely; invariant 4 is enforced via a token counter (`bounceToken`) rather than the bench's own boolean flag. Tests were written red-first: 7 of 8 new tests failed on the pre-port code; after the port, `playwright/carousel.spec.ts` is 65/65 and the tier-1 APG oracle is 11/11. **Test-methodology finding worth keeping on record:** `page.mouse.wheel(0, deltaY)` does not scroll a horizontal scroller in headless Chromium at all — `deltaX` does; a test asserting wheel-driven behaviour on a horizontal carousel needs the X axis regardless of how a real trackpad gesture would be expressed. **Open, stated plainly, not silently dropped:** the owner still needs to feel-test this on real hardware (mouse and trackpad, both axes, both edges) — synthetic wheel events cannot reproduce momentum, so this is not something Playwright can close out; the cross-browser rubber-band constant used is WebKit's own curve (row 103's measurement), unverified against Chromium's or Gecko's actual constants; arrow-paging while a bounce is already in flight is unexercised by any test; and there is no extra clipping wrapper around `CarouselContent` (it self-clips), which still wants a real-device visual check. **ADDENDUM 2026-09-25 (lane `carousel-rb-clip`), both flagged risks above materialized on the owner's real hardware, exactly as this row's own "still wants a real-device check"/"unverified against Chromium's or Gecko's" language warned they might.** (1) The "no extra clipping wrapper — `CarouselContent` self-clips" claim was wrong: an element's `overflow` clips relative to its OWN box, and a `transform` moves that box (clip region included) as one unit, so translating the scroller moved its clip boundary right along with it — a trackpad overdrag pushed the whole visible slide track ~750px past the carousel, unclipped, overlapping the rest of the page. Fixed by giving `CarouselContent` a stationary, presentational `div[data-slot="carousel-viewport"]` wrapper (`overflow: clip`, inline in the primitive itself, not `hidden` — a `hidden` box is still a scroll container, which would make the paging path's `scrollIntoView` scroll the wrapper too and permanently offset the content); both bridges now translate the scroller inside a clip boundary that never moves. (2) The port's own wheel-path curve constant was mis-transcribed: `L·x/(x+L)` with `L=d/c` is NOT algebraically identical to Apple's `x·c·d/(d+c·x)` as this row's own §4 source table claimed — they differ by a factor of `c` throughout (slope 1 vs `c` at `x=0`; asymptote `d/c`≈1.8·`d` vs `d`), so the shipped curve was roughly twice as sensitive and reached roughly twice the depth of the curve it was meant to port. Fixed in `CAROUSEL_WHEEL_BOUNCE_JS` only (`CAROUSEL_DRAG_JS`'s pointer-drag curve is untouched — the owner's report was trackpad-specific, and "mouse drag feels natural" as shipped). Both fixes and the corrected algebra are recorded in `dev-docs/research/carousel-overscroll-2026-09-23.md` §9's own dated entries. A separate, still-open concern from the same report — trackpad momentum overshoot needing damping beyond the curve fix — is deliberately NOT part of this addendum; the owner is deciding between that heuristic and a different construction, so it is being tracked and implemented separately rather than bundled in here. |
| 103 | Native sub-scroller rubber-band is real but **composited**, so it is invisible to `getBoundingClientRect`/`scrollLeft` — an instrumented observer records nothing and reads as "no bounce" | Measured 2026-09-23 with a script-free scroll-snap control added to the bench; the owner corrected two wrong conclusions I drew from its silence | N/A — a measurement limit to know, not work to schedule | I reported first "leave the wheel to the platform, macOS does this natively" (wrong — macOS gives elasticity to the *document*, not to an arbitrary sub-scroller) and then "there is no native rubber-band here at all" (also wrong). The owner settled it: *"there is an end rubber-band if there is still momentum coming from slide 2 to slide 1 and over, even if there is none from slide 1 to end."* The bounce exists when momentum arrives at an edge; it just never reaches a JS-readable layer. **The consequence is constructive:** it cannot be *measured* from inside the page, but it can be *modelled*, because the WebKit curve is published — `b(x) = (1 - 1/(x*c/d + 1))*d` with `c ~= 0.55`, algebraically identical to `L*x/(x+L)` with `L = d/c`. Other platform numbers from the same control, all reusable: rest after the last wheel event **64-78ms** typical (outliers 210/526/609ms on deliberately gentle gestures), device delta quantum **1px**, momentum decay **~0.96x per event**. All from one engine on one machine — Chromium and Gecko have their own curves, and a wrong constant degrades gracefully (stiffer or looser band) rather than breaking. |
| 104 | Instrumentation that keys off the wrong event, or records a threshold after it is reset, lies exactly where the bug is | Two telemetry defects found 2026-09-22 (bench v22) while chasing a B2 glitch that the data appeared to show nothing about | Done for the bench; worth remembering as a class | (a) Burst tracking keyed off **pad installation** rather than off the gesture, so a 49-second driving session recorded exactly **one** B2 burst — blind precisely in the region under investigation. (b) `releaseThreshold` was recorded **after** the peak it derives from had been zeroed, so every burst reported a flat `1.6` regardless of what was actually used. Both were fixed and the session re-driven. The general shape: instrumentation is code, it can be wrong, and a quiet dataset is evidence of nothing until the recorder itself has been checked against a case whose answer is already known. Related and separately instructive: the owner flagged *"1.6px floor might be wrong"* before any data did — it sat **below** the device's own 1px quantum, so it could never fire. It is now `deviceMinDelta * 2`, learned from the stream, with 1.6 as a fallback only until the device speaks. |
| 105 | `playwright/baseline.local.config.ts` (and `xvfb.local.config.ts`/`oracle.local.config.ts`/`ssg.local.config.ts`) lack the CI config's `grepInvert: /mobile/` on their chromium project, so every full local run also executes the `... (mobile)` specs and fails them on `.tap()` (needs `hasTouch`, which these launches don't emulate) | Found 2026-09-25 (round 8) in every full local run this session; reproduced identically on `f103bbd` (pre-existing, not caused by this round's own work) | **DONE 2026-09-25 (round 8)** | `navbar.spec.ts:26`, `select.spec.ts:132` and `sidebar.spec.ts:151` are each named with a `(mobile)` suffix and gated behind `grepInvert: /mobile/` in the CI config (`playwright/playwright.config.ts`), which the four `*.local.config.ts` files never copied — each defines its own single `chromium` project from scratch rather than extending the CI one. Fixed by adding the identical `grepInvert: /mobile/,` line to the `chromium` project in all four: `baseline.local.config.ts`, `xvfb.local.config.ts`, `oracle.local.config.ts`, `ssg.local.config.ts` — none of the four restrict `testDir`/`testMatch` to a subset of specs, so all four run the full suite and all four had the gap. No test file changed |
| 106 | This container's Chromium is `1194` (no `1223`, unlike some earlier sandboxes this repo's docs mention) — 15 `oracle/tier2-html/top-layer.spec.ts` rows fail against it | Found 2026-09-25 (round 8) running the full gate suite | Environment/engine class, not a product defect — no fix scheduled here; relates to row 21 | Rules 1 (DropdownMenu/Menubar), 4/5/6 (including the CALIBRATION rows, run against a bare native `<div popover>` with no library code involved at all), 8 (ColorPicker), and 11 (Popover with no anchor) — 15 rows total. All 15 reproduce byte-for-byte on unmodified `f103bbd`, so none are a regression from this round. The CALIBRATION rows failing against plain native markup is the decisive evidence: those rows exist specifically to establish what the browser itself does before comparing the library against it, so a CALIBRATION failure means the engine/environment doesn't behave the way the rule assumes, not that the library is wrong — this is an engine/environment class, per row 21's own already-established finding, not a new one |
| 107 | Two fresh-container cold-start traps in `scripts/check-attr-spread-collision.sh` and its `--update-baseline` mode | Found 2026-09-25 (round 8), first fresh-container run of the gate this session | Cosmetic; remedy known, not fixed here | (a) The analyzer is built with `cargo build --offline`, so on a genuinely fresh container (empty local registry cache) it fails with `failed to get \`dioxus-i18n\`` or similar until *some* other `cargo build` in the workspace has first populated `~/.cargo/registry`; running any ordinary `cargo check`/`cargo test` first, before the gate script, works around it. (b) `--update-baseline` reports `wrote 1 entries` even for a genuinely empty baseline — it is counting a trailing blank line in the `.tsv` output as a row. Neither affects the gate's actual detection logic; both are surface-level annoyances a fresh session should expect rather than mistake for a broken gate |
| 108 | Tests that locate rendered content by slicing an HTML string from a specific attribute-string match break when attribute order changes — the same class showed up twice this round (`calendar.rs`'s unit test, chart's `tooltip.rs::tooltip_fragment()`), both caused by row 93's `merge_attributes` construction sorting its output by attribute name | Found 2026-09-25 (round 8) while landing row 93; two occurrences in one round makes it a class per `CLAUDE.md`'s own rule, not two coincidences | Proposed, not built — record only, per this lane's own scope | **Root cause:** a test that does something like `html[html.find("aria-label=\"...\"")..]` is implicitly assuming a stable attribute order that nothing in Dioxus's own contract actually guarantees (`dioxus-core`'s own VNode data model has no ordering invariant on its dynamic-attribute slot to begin with — see row 93's root-cause account) — `merge_attributes` sorting by name was simply the first thing to make that latent assumption visible, and any future change to attribute construction order could do it again. **Proposed fix by construction, not per-instance:** a shared test helper (e.g. in a `playwright`/Rust-test-support module already used across specs) that parses a rendered start tag into an attribute name→value map — a real, if minimal, tag-attribute parser — rather than any test slicing a string match out of raw HTML. Every call site that currently slices from an attribute match would switch to `attrs.get("aria-label")`-style lookups, which are order-independent by construction and can never break this way again, regardless of what future attribute-ordering change lands. Two instances subsumed by this construction: `calendar.rs`'s unit test (was slicing from `aria-label="…"`) and chart's `tooltip.rs::tooltip_fragment()` (was slicing from a literal match instead of the tag's own `<`) — both were re-anchored on the tag start as an interim fix, not rewritten onto the proposed helper, so they remain individually correct but do not yet demonstrate the construction. Not built this round: this lane owns `dev-docs/**` and the four playwright config files only, not test-support code |
| 109 | SSG interaction tests race hydration — a click can land before wasm attaches Dioxus's delegated listeners; a hydration-ready signal fixes it (landed) | Found 2026-09-25 (round 8b) in the final integrated verification at `b557dfa`: an SSG run of `carousel.spec.ts` + the tier-1 carousel oracle + `hydration-parity.spec.ts` came back 146 passed / 3 failed — the deliberately-red `selftest:unfixed:id` plus two carousel tests that passed on `dx serve` but failed against the SSG build | S — one client-side effect, one shared Playwright helper, two call sites wired up; the sweep to the rest of the suite (below) is the open-ended part | **Evidence:** `carousel.spec.ts:633` — a Next click, sampled repeatedly, read `scrollLeft` 0 every time; the click hit prerendered markup before hydration had attached the delegated listeners, so the event was dropped outright, not queued for later. `waitUntil: "networkidle"` alone only got the flaky test to 4/5 — network-quiet is a different signal from hydrated, and no amount of retrying closes that gap. **Construction:** `preview/src/main.rs`'s `App` gained a signal-free one-shot `use_effect` running `document::eval("document.documentElement.dataset.hydrated = 'true'")`, client-only, after hydration commits — `<html>` is not vdom-owned, so this cannot affect SSR/hydration parity, confirmed by `hydration-parity.spec.ts` staying at 40/41 throughout. `playwright/hydration.ts` gained `gotoHydrated(page, url, opts)` (networkidle, then `waitForSelector('html[data-hydrated="true"]')`), wired into both carousel specs. `main.rs`'s own comment already pointed at "dev-docs/backlog.md: SSG interaction tests race hydration" before this row existed — this row is that pointer's target. **Fixed in `2235815`** (the signal itself) **and `bb51a02`** (both carousel specs switched onto `gotoHydrated`); confirmed by execution: the two previously-flaky tests 5/5 each at `--repeat-each=5`, the full `carousel.spec.ts` + tier-1 oracle 108/108 on both the SSG static serve and `dx serve`, `hydration-parity.spec.ts` unchanged at 40/41, all 12 `CLAUDE.md` gates green at `62183b3`. **Follow-up, not done — every other SSG-lane interaction spec still `goto`s directly and should migrate to `gotoHydrated` too:** `accordion-animation.spec.ts`, `date-picker.spec.ts`, `input_otp.spec.ts`, `oracle-focus-restore.spec.ts`, `oracle/tier1-apg/{keyboard-matrix,menu-roles,menu-submenu,select-only-combobox}.spec.ts`, `oracle/tier2-html/{form-label-association,form-participation,main-thread,native-dialog,top-layer,top-layer-ink,touch-double-tap,touch-focus-zoom}.spec.ts`, `oracle/tier3-radix/scroll-lock.spec.ts`, `pagination.spec.ts`, `pie_chart.spec.ts`, `radar_chart.spec.ts`, `radial_chart.spec.ts`, `select.spec.ts`, `tag_group.spec.ts`, plus `oracle/hydration-parity.spec.ts`'s own Rule 3 click. Row 90's `f468f78` `networkidle` fix for `pie_chart.spec.ts`/`radial_chart.spec.ts` is the same class under a weaker remedy and should move onto this helper too |
| 110 | Playwright's own actionability pre-scroll (`.hover()`/`.focus()`/`.click()`'s auto-scroll) animates under the site's global `html { scroll-behavior: smooth }` (`preview/assets/main.css:54`) — a class, confirmed at a second instance | Instance 2, 2026-09-25 (round 8b): `carousel.spec.ts`'s "hovering the carousel stops rotation…" failed roughly 3-4 times per 5-10 runs on the SSG build. Instance 1, 2026-09-18 (`daebe40`, confirmed by reading that commit: its own message states verbatim "html gets scroll-behavior: smooth so the jump animates"): `oracle/tier2-html/top-layer.spec.ts`'s `pinNearTop` helper (~line 1212) | Diagnosis M (two hypotheses tried and refuted before the real one); the by-construction fix is proposed, not built — S–M once someone picks an option | **Two hypotheses refuted, in order, before the real mechanism.** The main loop's own first read — a mid-transition autoplay tick counted as an extra advance — was disproved by a MutationObserver + `performance.now()` probe: `mouseenter` fires, a genuine `mouseleave` follows ~250-300ms later, and rotation resumes on a fresh ~1200ms cadence. The actual cause: the whole page was still smooth-scrolling (~900px) from `.hover()`'s own `scrollIntoViewIfNeeded` pre-check — an unqualified scroll defaults to `behavior: "auto"`, which itself defers to the ancestor's CSS `scroll-behavior: smooth` — dragging the carousel out from under the still-resting cursor. Confirmed two independent ways: forcing `scroll-behavior: auto` site-wide dropped it to 0/15 failures; an instant pre-scroll also gave 0/15. No `carousel.rs` change — component logic was correct throughout. **Fixed for instance 2** with a `scrollIntoViewInstant()` call before `.hover()` (and `.focus()`, as defence-in-depth) in `62183b3`. **Instance 1** (`top-layer.spec.ts`'s `pinNearTop`, per that helper's own inline comment dated 2026-09-18) predates this round and carries the identical fix, arrived at independently: `behavior: "instant"` on its `scrollIntoView`/`scrollTo` calls, opting out of the same CSS property for the same reason. **Per `CLAUDE.md`'s own "two occurrences is a class" rule — proposed here, not built** (this lane owns `dev-docs/**` only): a shared Playwright fixture, or an `addInitScript`/`page.addStyleTag` in the local + CI configs, setting `html { scroll-behavior: auto !important }` for the test run. This subsumes both instances above and any future actionability pre-scroll, without a per-call-site fix each time. It does **not** touch a test's own *explicit* `scrollIntoView({behavior: 'smooth'})` call — verified by reading `carousel.spec.ts`'s "paged transition animates" test, which asserts on exactly such a call: an explicit `behavior` argument overrides the ancestor CSS default regardless of what that CSS says, so this construction leaves animation tests working. **Weaker alternative:** scope `main.css`'s rule under `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)` — subsumes both instances too, but only for a runner that emulates `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, which this repo's configs do not do by default (confirmed: no `reducedMotion` project setting in any of the four `*.local.config.ts` files or in `playwright.config.ts`), so it would need that setting added as well to actually take effect |
| 111 | Migrate the theme to shadcn's system: semantic role tokens, then the Nova style | **Owner decision 2026-09-26: yes, backlogged** ("we need to migrate our theme to shadcn proper one") -- not started | M (phase A) + S/M (phase B foundations) + L (phase C, all components) | **Why:** the owner compared the carousel with shadcn's current docs (`/docs/components/base/carousel`) and the gap turned out to be theme-wide, not per-demo. shadcn's docs render the **Nova** style on ~95% of pages (`<ComponentPreview styleName="base-nova">` in `apps/v4/content/docs/components/{base,radix,aria}/*.mdx`, shadcn-ui/ui@98a1fe6); our theme was measured against the old un-prefixed pages and matches neither Nova nor Vega cleanly (radius ~2px tighter than both; Vega-like shadows, Nova-like density). **Mechanism gap (the bigger one):** shadcn has semantic role tokens (`--background`, `--foreground`, `--card`, `--popover`, `--primary(-foreground)`, `--secondary`, `--muted`, `--accent`, `--destructive`, `--border`, `--input`, `--ring`, `--chart-1..5`, `--sidebar-*`, `--radius` + derived radii; OKLCH; `.dark` override) defined once in `app/globals.css`, and a *style* (`registry/styles/style-nova.css`) only layers utility bundles on top -- two independent, swappable axes. We have no role layer: a numbered ramp (`--primary-color`, `-1..7`, `--secondary-color-*`, status colours; light/dark via the `var(--dark,…) var(--light,…)` space-toggle) referenced directly at ~424 sites in 64/72 component stylesheets, used inconsistently ("border" = 5 different steps; "surface" = all 8). Only focus ring and destructive are already semantic. **Upstream history (why the ramp exists):** DioxusLabs/components `315b62e` (2025-06-13, #51) created the theme with ad-hoc semantic names (`--background-color`, `--brighter-background-color`, `--muted-background-color`); `d414935` (2025-06-20, #66) renamed them to the numbered ramp, commit message "use more uniform names for the theme colors" -- naming hygiene in an early preview, not a rejection of roles. Worth keeping from upstream: the space-toggle mechanism (one declaration per token covers `data-theme`, `prefers-color-scheme` and non-browser renderers, no JS) and plain CSS (no Tailwind). **Plan:** (A) add `--dx-` role tokens with a shadcn-name fallback (e.g. `--dx-border: var(--border, <ramp step>)`) so a pasted shadcn theme works without colliding with a host app's own bare variables; repoint the ~424 sites by intended role, proven value-preserving with `playwright/computed-style-snapshot.spec.ts` (empty before/after diff); keep the ramp names as aliases for `dx components add` users. (B) Nova foundations: radius token swap (S), then card/button/input/badge/tabs/dialog/select shadow + density pass (M). (C) remaining components (L, high regression surface; axe colour-contrast re-run per the theme file's own rule). A switchable style (Nova/Vega presets) becomes a token-level choice only after (A). Research: `dev-docs/research/theme-2026-09-26/theme-vs-base-nova.md`, `shadcn-carousel-parity.md`. |

## What a fresh session should pick up next (2026-09-25)

1. **The user-gated rows are unchanged**: 4, 6, 14, 15, 22, 23, 28, 29, 30, 33, 40, 41b — none of them were sequenced this round and none became unblocked by it (verified against the table above, matching the 2026-09-18 list exactly).
2. **Carousel fast-follows are now LANDED (round 8b)** — `loop` (rewind-style, no cloned slides), autoplay + APG rotation control, and the tablist-picker variant all shipped; see row 91's own updated addendum. (Was: reported in progress by a separate lane as of the first round-8 docs pass — confirmed landed, not just started, by this pass.)
3. **Row 102's own open item**: the owner still needs to feel-test the carousel edge rubber-band on real hardware (mouse and trackpad, both axes, both edges) — nothing synthetic can substitute for that, per this round's own account.
4. **Firefox/WebKit calibration (row 4)** remains not done — this session, like several before it, had only Chromium available; a fresh session should check its own `/opt/pw-browsers` contents rather than assume otherwise.
5. **Optional RTL for `navigation_menu`/`drawer`/`command`/`date_picker`** (row 13's 2026-09-25 correction) — confirmed *not* a shadcn-parity gap, so this is a beyond-parity nice-to-have, not something blocking any parity claim.
6. **The test-helper construction from row 108** — a shared attribute-map parser, replacing string-slicing tests that assume attribute order — is proposed but not built; worth doing before the next construction that touches attribute ordering finds a third instance.
7. **Chart stage 2's browser + SSG validation is now DONE (round 8b)** — row 90's own addendum has the numbers (102/102 on the two fixed pie/radial specs, 80-page SSG release build, 0 base-path refs, hydration-parity 40/41). Treat the galleries as fully verified, not just gate-green. (Was: reported in progress separately as of the first round-8 docs pass.)
8. **New — migrate the remaining SSG-lane interaction specs onto `gotoHydrated`** (row 109's own follow-up list): `accordion-animation.spec.ts`, `date-picker.spec.ts`, `input_otp.spec.ts`, `oracle-focus-restore.spec.ts`, four `oracle/tier1-apg/*` specs, eight `oracle/tier2-html/*` specs, `oracle/tier3-radix/scroll-lock.spec.ts`, `pagination.spec.ts`, `pie_chart.spec.ts`, `radar_chart.spec.ts`, `radial_chart.spec.ts`, `select.spec.ts`, `tag_group.spec.ts`, and `oracle/hydration-parity.spec.ts`'s own Rule 3 click — each is presumed to share the same click-before-hydration exposure the carousel specs actually hit, until proven otherwise per spec.
9. **New — pick and build the scroll-behavior-smooth construction from row 110**: a shared Playwright fixture/`addInitScript` (or `page.addStyleTag`) forcing `html { scroll-behavior: auto !important }` during tests, which subsumes both confirmed instances (carousel's autoplay-hover test, `top-layer.spec.ts`'s `pinNearTop`) and prevents a third. Verify the "explicit smooth-scroll assertions keep working" claim by execution, not just by reading, before landing it.

Standing rule for every item: the definition-of-done loop from [`plan.md`](./plan.md) — rule-cited oracle red first, provenance per `lifting-from-forks.md` §1, checks green, ledger updated.
